Where: data model `agreement`, flow steps 6–7, "Agreements: creation and signing", Phase 8, Verification.
3. **R6 #3 (High) signed-agreement lifecycle** — *Andy proposal, for Bob re-review.* A **signed** agreement (MSA included) is never voided and never edited; a correction is a new agreement that **supersedes** it (new status `superseded`, `supersedes_id`). Only unsigned agreements may be `void`. Signed SOWs keep pointing at the MSA they were signed under; new SOWs link to the customer's current signed, non-superseded MSA; unsigned SOWs frozen against an MSA that gets superseded are voided in the same transaction and regenerated. `signed_at` is set once and never cleared, which is what the "MSA signed" lock argument relies on. New event `agreement.superseded`. Where: data model `agreement`, Immutability rules, "Agreements: creation and signing".
4. **R6 #4 (High) purge vs later invoice** — *Andy proposal, for Bob re-review.* A version is purgeable only if **no invoice (draft or issued) references it and it has no retention record**. New append-only `version_retention` table (the durable `retained_at` flag, kept out of the version tables so they stay free of UPDATE grants); new `invoice.source_quotation_version_id` / `source_proposal_sent_version_id`. Invoice creation from a quote or payment schedule, and issuance, take `FOR SHARE` on the **parent** `quotation` / `proposal` row and record the reference + retention row; the purge job takes `FOR UPDATE` on the same parent row and re-checks inside the lock, so issuance and purge serialise. (Parent row, not version row: `FOR SHARE` needs UPDATE privilege, which nobody has on version tables.) Narrow purge **scope written as a default pending Charlie**, incl. agreements retained. New concurrency test. Where: data model (versions, `invoice`, `version_retention`), "Audit, logging, privacy", Phase 9, Verification, Open question 5.
5. **R6 #5 (Medium) system-module authorization tests** — *Andy proposal, for Bob re-review.* Two-workspace tests that every system-module read and mutation on `organization`, `member`, `invitation` and the other Better Auth tables enforces user, membership and role, incl. cross-workspace attempts by a member of one workspace only; the no-RLS allow-list test is stated as necessary but not sufficient. Where: Tenancy, Phase 1 done-when, Verification.
6. **R6 #6 (Medium) refunds made atomic** — *Andy proposal, for Bob re-review.* Exactly one source column, `payment_id NOT NULL` (the `credit_note_id` source is removed; money going back after a credit note is a refund against the payment it returns, citing the credit note in its reason). Currency must equal the payment's. Append-only except a one-time void (`voided_at`, `voided_by`, `void_reason`); voided refunds don't count. Cap Σ non-voided refunds ≤ payment amount, enforced under `invoice` → `payment` `FOR UPDATE` with a re-sum; invoice status recalculated in the same transaction. New event `refund.voided`. Concurrency test. Where: data model `refund`, "Refunds and invoice status", Phase 5, Verification.

Also in revision 3 (from Andy's rulings on Bob's section verdicts): agreement parent integrity (same workspace, same customer, right parent type: composite FK + immutable-column trigger + CHECKs, with a test); customer pattern change **default pending Charlie** (future approvals only; blocked while provisioning is pending); Bob's recommendation on `pg` added next to Open question 1 (not decided); `crm_worker` exact grants and the full pg-boss maintenance-cycle test stated as Phase 1 gates.

## Changes in v4.5 revision 2 (on top of the first v4.5 draft)

*(Revision 3 refines items 3 and 4: the approval transaction no longer looks up the MSA, and `superseded` joins the agreement statuses.)*

1. **Agreements: MSA + SOW kept, NDA deferred** (Charlie's decision, relayed; reverses the first draft's cut). Part 1 types: Service Agreement, MSA, SOW, Change order, Sign-off. NDA becomes a later template on the same engine (no redesign). Only v4.4 flow step 2 (NDA) is removed; the flow is **13 steps** (v4.4 had 14).
2. **Agreement pattern moves to the customer**: new `customer.agreement_pattern` (`service_agreement` | `msa_sow`); `workspace_settings.default_agreement_pattern` is the default for new customers. v4.4 had it per workspace only.
3. **Approval transaction simplified**: it creates exactly one agreement (Service Agreement, or a SOW pointing at the customer's signed MSA or at none) and emits `agreement.created`. v4.4's "MSA-if-missing" creation inside the locked approval transaction is replaced by a post-commit job that creates a draft MSA (one open MSA per customer, unique partial index) and links the SOW. Lock order unchanged.
4. **SOW signing requires a signed MSA**: the signing link signs the MSA first, then the SOW, in one OTP session. `parent_msa_id` is kept and used; new `agreement.parent_agreement_id` lets change orders and sign-offs reference the SOW (`msa_sow` customers) or the Service Agreement; `agreement.status` gains `void`. New event `agreement.created`. Phase 8 done-when and Verification add: one MSA under concurrent approvals, SOW blocked until MSA signed, race test still passing.
5. **Spike results recorded** (both PASSED, 2026-09-29): "Checks before the Phase 1 plan" now holds results, versions and decisions instead of conditions.
6. **Transactional enqueue is the design**: `boss.send(…, { db: fromDrizzle(tx, sql), id: event.id })` inside the business transaction. The outbox + relay fallback, `domain_event.relayed_at`, its index and the relay job are **dropped** (one history line kept).
7. **`nullif` in every policy is required**, backed by a policy lint/test; RLS tests must seed rows in other workspaces (the missing-`nullif` error appears only when rows exist).
8. **postgres.js `prepare: false`** on the app client (non-overridable when `DATABASE_URL` points at a pooler).
9. **`pgboss` grants for `crm_app`** recorded (USAGE; SELECT on `version`, `queue`; INSERT + column SELECT on `job_common`); `crm_app` still cannot read job payloads. Grants are per queue table (`ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` or re-grant per queue). pg-boss installed and upgraded by `crm_migrator` in migrations; app instances run with `migrate/createSchema: false`.
10. **Fifth role `crm_worker`** (spike recommendation): owns the worker-side pg-boss rights (fetch, complete, maintenance) on `pgboss.*`; exact grants measured in Phase 1.
11. **Pools and connections**: starting pool sizes recorded; pooler must be transaction mode; LISTEN/NOTIFY needs a direct connection; queue `notify` stays off in Phase 1 (polling) and `notify` changes happen only in migrations.
12. **Known risks updated**: R1 confirmed and fixed; untested items added (pg-boss maintenance through the pooler over a full cycle, `crm_worker` exact grants, DO Managed PG pooler run, 60 s web queue-cache lag). `pg`-driver question stays open, now noting the web-side pg-boss instance opens a small `pg` pool.

## Changes in v4.5 (from v4.4) — merge with the parallel "v4" draft

*(Items 5, 9 and 11 are superseded by revision 2 above; they are shown as corrected.)*

All v4.1–v4.4 fixes are kept: `document_artifact` (no `pdf_key` anywhere), Resend + `crm_link_resolver`, lock order `proposal` → `share_link` → `quotation`, `clock_timestamp()`, exact money rules (3-decimal quantities, worked examples), Phase 4 first invoice / Phase 5 first quote, Node 24 LTS, `crm_app` INSERT + SELECT on append-only tables. Where the other draft disagreed on these, v4.4 wins.

1. **`account` → `billing_account`** (bug): Better Auth creates its own `account` table; the billing owner table would collide in Phase 1.
2. **Workspace context set with `select set_config('app.workspace_id', $1, true)`** (bug): `SET LOCAL` cannot take a bind parameter. Policies use `workspace_id = (select nullif(current_setting('app.workspace_id', true), '')::uuid)`: the sub-select reads the setting once per query, and `nullif` makes a reused pooled connection return 0 rows instead of failing on `''::uuid`.
3. **New role `crm_purge`** (no `BYPASSRLS`; four roles in the first draft, five after revision 2 adds `crm_worker`): may DELETE only `quotation_version` / `proposal_sent_version` rows that never led to an invoice (rule made exact and race-free in revision 3: no invoice reference, no retention record, parent-row locking), plus their `document_artifact` rows and files. Issued documents are retained. Reason: PDPA purge vs append-only tables.
4. **Better Auth tables get no RLS**: reached only through the allow-listed system module, guarded by the lint rule. Workspace config (bank details, DuitNow QR, own tax IDs, branding, country/currency/tax defaults) moves off Better Auth's `organization` into a new **`workspace_settings`** table under RLS, so sensitive data never sits in a table without RLS.
5. **Events → jobs**: pg-boss job enqueued in the same transaction as the change (`send` with a `db` executor), replacing the v4.4 outbox + relay (Spike 2 passed; the fallback is dropped in revision 2). `domain_event` stays as append-only history. Job payloads carry IDs only (the `pgboss` tables have no RLS).
6. **Extension system moved to Part 3 (SaaS)**: contract, `ctx`, `workspace_extension`, fan-out dispatcher, `_fixture` extension and Settings → Extensions page are no longer built in Part 1. The v4.4 text is kept as design in **Appendix A**. Part 1 keeps domain events and the e-invoice stub columns. Adds the three-test extension rule and the payments split (recording payments, receipts and refunds is core; collecting money is an extension).
7. **Hosting staged**: laptop (Phases 0–3) → one Singapore VPS behind Cloudflare Tunnel, nightly encrypted backups to R2 (Phase 4 on) → DigitalOcean Managed PostgreSQL, Singapore (at first sale). Replaces v4.4's "Fly.io / Railway / Hetzner, Neon allowed". Cloudflare Workers/Pages, Cloudflare Containers, Neon free and Supabase free listed as rejected with reasons.
8. **PostgreSQL 17** (18 allowed), was "16+". Dev and tests pin 17.
9. **Agreements** (corrected in revision 2): **NDA deferred** to a later template; **MSA and SOW kept**; v4.4 flow **step 2 (NDA) removed**, so the flow is **13 steps** and the full E2E is the 13-step flow; the approval transaction is simplified and the agreement pattern is per customer (see revision 2, items 1–4). The first draft's cut of MSA, SOW and `parent_msa_id` is withdrawn.
10. **New "Business and sequencing" section**: own use first, Gates A–D with measurable exit criteria, demo rounds instead of design partners, Sdn Bhd at first sale, part order 1 → 3 → 2 → 4, "quote-to-cash for agencies" positioning.
11. **Checks before the Phase 1 plan**: Spike 1 (RLS + `set_config` through a transaction-mode pool), Spike 2 (pg-boss `send` inside the Drizzle transaction), and "host/provider chosen". Both spikes passed on 2026-09-29 (revision 2); Phase 0 done-when records "spikes passed".
12. **Phase scope merged**: the other draft's phase scope with v4.4's done-when tests kept; target dates added as estimates only.
13. **Phase 5 trimmed**: adds **recurring invoices** and **manual refunds**. **Removed from Part 1 phases**: customer credit balance and statement of account (never in v4.4; proposed by the other draft) → "after Gate A, only if own use needs them, as derived views". Reason: closest to building an accounting system.
14. **Malaysian market**: Share to WhatsApp (`wa.me`, OTP still required) on client links; after a Resend the UI offers "Share new link to WhatsApp" as well as the email. DuitNow QR on invoices, stored content-addressed and never deleted; the invoice snapshot references its hash key. CSV import of customers and contacts (Phase 3).
15. **Own SST/GST registration no., TIN and BRN** on `workspace_settings`. SST/GST question stays default **No** (v4.4 text kept, incl. SG GST 9%).
16. **`fx_rate`**: manual entry, defaulting to the last rate used for that currency pair in the workspace.
17. **PDPA-safe audit log**: personal-data fields in before/after JSON stored as `"[pii]"`; `domain_event` payloads carry IDs and amounts only.
18. **Product additions**: `notification` table (Phase 1); pipeline board and proposal branding (Phase 6); owner notifications, mobile-first client pages, Turnstile on OTP requests (Phase 7); workspace offboarding and customer purge (Phase 9). Outbound webhooks and Google login move to Part 3.
19. **"Later features" no longer lists recurring invoices** (now Phase 5).
20. **Line discounts stay percentage-only** (bps); a fixed-amount discount exists only at document level. The other draft's per-line fixed amount is not merged.
21. **Known risks section added**: empty-string setting → `nullif`; `pgboss` tables have no RLS; `pg` driver brought by pg-boss (open); connection budget and direct connections; exact pins for pg-boss and drizzle.

### Open questions for Charlie

1. **RESOLVED at the v4.5 approval** (item 3 of the Approval record above (Charlie, 2026-09-30, v4.5 at `8989d59`)). *Original text:* **`pg` driver.** pg-boss depends on `pg` (8.23.0 with pg-boss 12.35.0) and always opens its own node-postgres pool — in the worker **and** in the web-side instance used only for `send` (a small pool, `max: 2`, that reads `pgboss.version` / `pgboss.queue`; Spike 2). The transactional `send` itself runs through the app's postgres.js transaction. The Phase 0 plan says "`postgres` driver only, never `pg`". Accept `pg` only as pg-boss's transitive dependency (the spike note recommends this; app code stays on `postgres`), or give pg-boss a postgres.js-backed `db` adapter (loses LISTEN and transactional workers)? **Bob's recommendation (R6):** allow `pg` only inside pg-boss and keep all app queries on postgres.js, because that matches the tested design. *Resolution:* Charlie accepted that recommendation: `pg` is allowed only inside pg-boss; all app queries stay on postgres.js.
2. **WhatsApp for invoices in Phase 4.** The other draft puts "send by email + Share to WhatsApp" in Phase 4, but share links and `resolve_share_link()` arrive in Phase 7. This draft puts Share to WhatsApp in Phase 7 for all client links; Phase 4 invoices go by email with the PDF. Alternative: pull a minimal invoice link page (and the resolver) into Phase 4.
3. **Part 2 scope.** The other draft cuts the Gantt editor permanently (Part 2 = milestone tracker + read-only timeline + one-way `projects-sync` extension). Not among the relayed picks, so v4.4's Part 2 text is kept with a note.
4. **VPS provider for Phase 4** (host/provider chosen line): **RESOLVED (Charlie, 2026-10-15): Hostinger KVM 2, Malaysia** ("Changes in v4.7", item 7; `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-10-15). The Stage 3 managed-database provider is not part of this question and is chosen at first sale.
5. **RESOLVED at the v4.5 approval** (item 1 of the Approval record above (Charlie, 2026-09-30, v4.5 at `8989d59`)). *Original text:* **Purge scope.** The spec now writes a narrow default ("Audit, logging, privacy" → Customer purge): purge only versions that never led to an invoice, plus their `document_artifact` rows and files, and the share links, OTP challenges and signatures attached to those versions; redact customer and contact rows only if the customer has **no** issued documents (signed agreements count as issued); customers with issued documents keep their rows. **Agreements attached to a never-invoiced approved proposal are retained, not purged** (a signed agreement is a contract), and so are that proposal's approved sent version and its approval signature, which the agreement rests on. Confirm or change: what is the purge meant to remove? *Resolution:* Charlie accepted the narrow default written above.
6. **Workspace offboarding retention.** What deletion removes vs keeps, given issued documents are retained for the statutory period (~7 years, to verify)?
7. **Part 3 first payment gateway.** The other draft names `payments-stripe` (Stripe Connect on SaaS, restricted key on self-host); v4.4 left Stripe / Billplz / Curlec to the Part 3 spec. Kept open here.
8. **RESOLVED (Charlie, 2026-10-20; Phase 1 plan): own login** (`crm_purge LOGIN`, no member roles, `DATABASE_PURGE_URL` only in the worker from Phase 9). Charlie's words: "follow bob's suggestions", adopting Bob's recommended answer "ASK 1: own login." (Phase 1 plan ASK 1 and Settled item 1; `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-10-20; revision 8, "Changes in v4.7" item 9). *Original text:* **How `crm_purge` connects** (own login + env var, or `SET ROLE` from the worker). To settle in the Phase 1 plan with Bob.
9. **Turnstile on self-host.** It needs a Cloudflare account; this draft makes it on by default on SaaS and optional on self-host.
10. **RESOLVED at the v4.5 approval** (item 2 of the Approval record above (Charlie, 2026-09-30, v4.5 at `8989d59`)). *Original text:* **Customer agreement-pattern change.** Default written into the spec: a change to `customer.agreement_pattern` affects **future approvals only**; existing agreements stay as they are; the change is **refused while a provisioning job for that customer is pending** (any unfrozen SOW for the customer). Confirm or change. *Resolution:* Charlie accepted this default, both the future-approvals-only rule and the refusal while a provisioning job is pending (scope confirmed by Charlie on 2026-10-09; Approval record item 2).
11. **RESOLVED at the v4.5 approval** (item 4 of the Approval record above (Charlie, 2026-09-30, v4.5 at `8989d59`)). *Original text:* **Refund eligibility.** Andy proposal written into the spec: a refund is allowed only up to the invoice's refundable excess, `max(0, net_paid − (invoice_total − issued_credit_notes_total))`, or the full net paid if the invoice is void, and never more than the payment. A broader refund policy (e.g. goodwill refunds on a fully paid invoice, which would need a credit note first under this rule) was not chosen. *Resolution:* Charlie accepted the excess-only rule. A broader policy would be a new owner decision.
12. **RESOLVED at the v4.5 approval** (item 5 of the Approval record above (Charlie, 2026-09-30, v4.5 at `8989d59`)). *Original text:* **Customer-facing label for credited invoices — default "Credited".** The status `credited` (settled entirely by credit notes, no net payment retained) is shown as "Credited" in the invoice list and on client-facing views unless Charlie picks another label. *Resolution:* Charlie accepted "Credited".
13. **RESOLVED at the v4.5 approval** (item 6 of the Approval record above (Charlie, 2026-09-30, v4.5 at `8989d59`)). *Original text:* **Allowing a credit-note void that leaves the client owing after a refund — default refuse.** The spec refuses such a void (the owner is not told to void the refund; a refund record may be voided only if it is wrong and either no money was returned or a same-transaction replacement records the actual outflow); allowing it would reopen a debt the client was already refunded against. *Resolution:* Charlie accepted the refusal. Allowing such a void would be a new owner decision.
14. **RESOLVED in v4.6 revision 2** (owner decision, Charlie 2026-10-03: "yes to both", adopting Bob's recommendation; `docs/DECISIONS.md`). *Original text:* **Currency columns not decided in v4.6 — open for the spec review (MON-02).** v4.6 decides only the `service` currency (see "Changes in v4.6"). Still open: (a) currency on `line_item`; (b) currency on `credit_note`; (c) the stored currency code for the frozen base totals. Nothing is assumed here. *Resolution:* (a) stored `line_item.currency`, enforced equal to the parent's currency; (b) stored `credit_note.currency`, copied from and enforced equal to the invoice's; (c) a frozen `base_currency` in every finalized base-total snapshot. The decision now lives in "Changes in v4.6 revision 2" items 4–6, data model `line_item`, `credit_note`, `quotation_version`, `invoice`, `workspace_settings`, Money rules ("Frozen base currency", "Draft currency change"), Tenancy (polymorphic reference triggers) and Verification.
15. **RESOLVED in v4.6 revision 3** (owner decision, Charlie 2026-10-03: "yes" to Bob's A, A, B — option B for this question; `docs/DECISIONS.md`). *Original text:* **Dashboard and period export when documents carry different frozen base currencies — open (MON-05).** Each finalized document keeps its own frozen `base_currency` (v4.6 revision 2), and `workspace_settings.base_currency` can change later, so issued documents may carry different frozen base currencies. MON-05 forbids summing amounts in different currencies. What Dashboard v1 and the period export show in that case is not decided. Nothing is assumed here. *Resolution:* totals are shown separately per frozen base currency, never summed across them and never converted between base currencies; the current workspace base currency is shown first; export rows carry their own frozen `base_currency` and `fx_rate`; the pipeline board uses frozen version base totals for sent items and, for drafts, a base value only when the draft holds a valid `fx_rate`. The decision now lives in "Changes in v4.6 revision 3" item 3, Dashboard v1, data model "Pipeline board", Accounting handoff and Verification.
16. **RESOLVED in v4.6 revision 4; rule replaced in revision 5** (owner decisions, Charlie 2026-10-05: Decision 2 = A, reconcile; then Decision B = 1 on Bob review R4, cumulative rounding; `docs/DECISIONS.md`). *Original text:* **Rounding across rows of one invoice — default pending Charlie (MON-04, MON-06).** Default written into the spec (v4.6 revision 3): each credit note, payment and refund is converted to base currency on its own (invoice's frozen `fx_rate`, step 9 formula, rounded once), and the stored per-row base amounts are what the dashboard and period export sum. There is **no forced reconciliation** to the invoice's base total, so an invoice paid in full in several payments can show base "paid" figures that differ from its base total by a few minor units (see the rounding fixture in Verification). Alternative: settle the last payment (the one that brings `net_paid` to `amount_owed`) against the remainder of the invoice's base total instead of converting it on its own. Confirm or change. *Resolution (revision 5, cumulative rounding; replaces revision 4's reconciliation, where the settling row took the remainder and could be negative):* each row stores the change in a converted running total. A payment stores `conv(net_paid_after) − conv(net_paid_before)`, a refund `conv(net_paid_before) − conv(net_paid_after)`, and a credit note's base total `conv(amount_owed_before) − conv(amount_owed_after)`. Each is measured against the stored sums and floored at 0, which matters only after a void. No row is negative (database CHECKs). An invoice settled with no excess has `B_net + B_cn = B_inv` exactly, and Dashboard v1 shows it with base outstanding 0. The one exception is the void edge case in Money rules. The decision now lives in "Changes in v4.6 revision 5" item 2, Money rules ("Base amounts for credit notes, payments and refunds"), data model `payment`, `refund`, `credit_note`, Dashboard v1, Phase 5 and Verification (j), (k), (n), (p), (q).

## Changes in v4.4 (from v4.3) — Bob final check

1. **Resolver declared `VOLATILE`** (final #1): its policy uses `clock_timestamp()`, so `STABLE` was the wrong promise.
2. **Resend approval source recorded** (final #2): Charlie approved it directly in the Andy session on 2026-09-29; Bob's window did not see that message.
3. **Orphan sweep runs per workspace** (final optional): one sub-job per workspace under `withWorkspace()`, listing only that workspace's `pdf/` prefix.

## Changes in v4.3 (from v4.2) — Bob review R4 (approve with conditions)

1. **Expiry checked with wall-clock time** (R4 medium): the approval recheck uses `clock_timestamp()` after the locks are held, not `now()`; the resolver policy and OTP window use it too. New expiry-under-lock test.
2. **Grant wording aligned** (R4 low): `crm_app` may INSERT and SELECT on append-only tables, never UPDATE or DELETE.
3. **Orphan PDF sweep** (R4 optional): rendered PDFs use the `ws/{workspace_id}/pdf/` prefix; a daily job deletes files there older than 24 h with no `document_artifact` row.
4. **Resend approved by Charlie** (recorded in Decided points).

## Changes in v4.2 (from v4.1) — Bob review R3

1. **PDFs recorded in `document_artifact`** (R3 #1): new append-only table keyed to the immutable version/document. No table has `pdf_key` any more, so the worker records a PDF by INSERT only, and version and signature rows stay untouched. Retries are insert-once.
2. **Approval vs Resend locking** (R3 #2): a fixed lock order `proposal` → `share_link` → `quotation`; approve rechecks inside the lock that the link is live and names `current_sent_version_id`; Resend is refused after approval. A required approve-vs-Resend race test is added to Phase 7.
3. **Role count** (R3 low): Decisions table and Phase 1 now say three roles (`crm_migrator`, `crm_app`, `crm_link_resolver`), none with `BYPASSRLS`.

## Changes in v4.1 (from v4) — Bob review R2

1. **Link resolver works under `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`** (R2 #1): no RLS bypass. A `NOLOGIN` role `crm_link_resolver` owns `resolve_share_link()` and gets one `SELECT` policy on `share_link` only. No role has `BYPASSRLS`. Tests use the real roles.
2. **FX conversion handles currency exponents** (R2 #2): `fx_rate` defined as major-per-major; `base_minor = round(doc_minor × fx_rate × 10^(exp_base − exp_doc))`; JPY, USD and KWD fixtures.
3. **Quote versions stored** (R2 #3): new append-only `quotation_version`; each proposal sent version points to one quote version; approval sets `quotation.accepted_version_id` to that version; unsent edits move to a new draft quote.
4. **One old-link behaviour** (R2 #4): unknown, revoked, expired and superseded links all show the same generic page; the client gets the new link by email on resend.
5. **Append-only made literal** (R2 optional): `proposal_sent_version` and `quotation_version` have no mutable columns and no UPDATE/DELETE grants; "superseded" and "approved" live on the parent row.

## Changes in v4 (from v3)

1. **Phase gates fixed** (Bob #1): Phase 4 onboarding gate is now "first *invoice* sent within 5 minutes"; "first *quote* sent within 5 minutes" moves to Phase 5, where quotes are built.
2. **Sent links are version-bound** (Bob #2): the proposal editor still shows the linked quote live while drafting. Sending freezes a *sent version* of proposal + quote; the link shows only that version. A changed quote requires **Resend**, which creates a new version and a new link and revokes the old one. Approval is only possible on the current sent version.
3. **Anonymous link access specified** (Bob #3): a narrow `resolve_share_link()` lookup resolves token → workspace + one target document, then all reads run under `withWorkspace()` for that workspace. Tests prove a token cannot reach another document or workspace.
4. **No tax by default** (Bob #4): sign-up asks "Are you registered for SST / GST?". Only a "yes" turns on tax (MY service tax 8%, SG GST 9% preselected, editable). Otherwise documents carry no tax row.
5. **Money rules made exact** (Bob #5): quantity precision, discount types, pro-rata document-discount allocation (largest remainder), per-rate-group tax rounding, and a worked mixed-rate example.
6. **E-invoice wording qualified** (Bob #6): the RM3M exemption is "generally", with all Guideline v4.8 §1.6.10 exceptions listed, and all of them added to the accountant check.

## Changes in v3 (from v2)

1. **Target segment fixed**: small and medium creative agencies, roughly 5–15 people, annual turnover under RM3 million. This decides what is refused: no accounting, no e-invoice integration in Part 1, no enterprise features.
2. **Extension architecture added** (modular monolith with extension seams). Core defines typed interfaces, domain events and a `ctx` API; extensions are packages in this repo, compiled into the same image, enabled per workspace as data. Part 1 builds the contract and ships with an empty extension list. No runtime plugin loading.
3. **E-invoice reduced to a stub**: customer TIN/BRN/SST fields, `einvoice_*` fields on invoice and credit note, PDF rendered at send gated on e-invoice status, and the `EInvoiceProvider` interface. The Malaysia implementation (`einvoice-my`) is a future extension, not a roadmap part. MSIC/classification codes removed.
4. **Monorepo layout** (pnpm workspaces): `packages/core`, `packages/extensions/*`, `apps/web`. Boundary rules become package boundaries.
5. **Domain events via transactional outbox** (`domain_event` written in the same transaction as the change, relayed to pg-boss). Core handlers and extension handlers both consume them.
6. **`organization.country`** added; drives tax defaults, formats and extension availability. **`workspace_extension`** table added with AES-GCM-encrypted settings.
7. **PDF timing changed**: rendered by a job at first send (invoices only after `einvoice_status ∈ {not_required, validated}`), stored, never regenerated. Draft previews are watermarked and not stored.
8. **Onboarding is a requirement**: MY/SG defaults, first quote sent within 5 minutes of sign-up (Phase 4 done-when). *v4: invoice in Phase 4, quote in Phase 5.*
9. **Agreement templates**: a single "Service Agreement" is the default; MSA + SOW remain available.
10. **Accounting handoff**: period export (CSV/JSON) of invoices, payments and credit notes in Part 1; API sync is a later `AccountingSync` extension.
11. **Dashboard v1** specified (Phase 5), in workspace base currency.
12. **Part 3 first extension is payment links** (Stripe / Billplz / Curlec, chosen in the Part 3 spec). **Part 4** carries license entitlements per extension and must re-confirm who the self-host buyer is.

## Changes in v2 (from v1) — kept for history

One-runtime Docker image (`web` + `worker`); pg-boss jobs instead of outbox + external cron; RLS specified mechanically; OTP-verified signatures with certificate page; money in `bigint` minor units; `account` above workspace; `DEPLOYMENT_MODE`; timezone/locale, per-line tax, discounts, email templates, invitations, optimistic locking, bank-details snapshot, workspace export; threat model, permission matrix, step-up auth, shared rate limits, session hygiene, upload rules, append-only audit, supply-chain scanning, restore drill, PDPA path, DMARC; phases reordered into a vertical slice (real invoice in Phase 4); Node 22 + pnpm, Tailwind + shadcn/ui, Testcontainers, Pino + Sentry (opt-in on self-host), nonce CSP in Phase 1, Ed25519 licensing, Stripe for Part 3.

---

## Context

Charlie runs a creative/dev studio (branding, copywriting, UI/UX, development). This CRM is built as a **product** and dogfooded by the studio from Phase 4 onward. It will be sold two ways:

- **Hosted SaaS** (Charlie's paid platform, scales by running more containers).
- **Self-host**: the buyer runs the same Docker image on their own cloud server and owns their data. Setup fee + one-off license + optional support/updates subscription. *Open question for Part 4: small agencies don't run servers, so the self-host buyer is likely a more technical sub-segment; confirm before investing.*

### Target segment

Small and medium creative/dev agencies: roughly 5–15 people, annual turnover under RM3 million, Malaysia first, Singapore second, other countries only where nothing country-specific is required. Consequences:

- It is a **CRM for selling and getting paid** ("quote-to-cash for agencies"), not an accounting system. A tax row on documents is sufficient; ledgers, journals, chart of accounts and period close are out of scope permanently. Accounting software receives data via export (Part 1) or sync (later extension).
- **E-invoicing is not built in Part 1.** Businesses under the current Malaysian exemption threshold (RM3M as of Guideline v4.8, 30 Aug 2026; the threshold has moved four times in two years) are *generally* exempt, **unless an exception in Guideline v4.8 §1.6.10 applies**: non-individual shareholders, a qualifying holding company, or related companies / joint ventures at or above the threshold. Some target customers will therefore need e-invoicing even under RM3M. The system keeps a cheap seam so it can be added as an extension when a customer needs it.
- **Onboarding must be trivial**: sensible defaults, first invoice sent within 5 minutes of sign-up (Phase 4), first quote within 5 minutes (Phase 5).
- Multi-currency stays (agencies bill USD/SGD often enough). Workspaces stay (Charlie needs them). Roles stay.

The product is split into 4 sub-projects, each with its own spec → plan → build cycle, **built in the order 1 → 3 → 2 → 4**:

1. **Core Sales** (this spec)
2. Projects: Gantt chart, fed by the proposal timeline block *(scope open; see Open question 3)*
3. SaaS layer: sign-up, plans, billing per `billing_account` (covering N workspaces); extension contract (Appendix A) built with its first consumer, a payment-links extension
4. Self-host pack: Docker kit, signed offline license with extension entitlements, private registry updates gated by support subscription, buyer setup guide, setup-fee service. Built only once 3 buyers have prepaid.

Folder: `/Users/charlie/Documents/Projects/Personal/CRM`.

## Business and sequencing

- **Own use first, then sell.** The studio (sole proprietorship) runs on the product from Phase 4. Selling starts only when the stability exit criteria (Gate D) are met.
- **Sdn Bhd at first sale**: incorporated before any other agency's data enters the system.
- **Outside signal without selling**: demo rounds with 5 agency owners on Charlie's own screen (no accounts, no data), after Phase 5 and again after Phase 8. Replaces design partners. Answers recorded in `docs/research/demos.md`.
- **Positioning**: "quote-to-cash for agencies", not "CRM". Pipeline board in Phase 6.

### Release gates

| Gate | When | Exit criteria |
|---|---|---|
| A — Own use starts | End of Phase 4 | Studio issues all its invoices and receipts through the product; nightly backup to R2 running; one restore tested. |
| B — Demo round 1 | End of Phase 5 | 5 agency owners have seen a demo; answers in `docs/research/demos.md`; price hypothesis tested. |
| C — Demo round 2 | End of Phase 8 | At least 3 say they would pay the hypothesised price. |
| D — Stability exit | After Phase 9 and at least 8 weeks of own use | 8 consecutive weeks with all studio documents through the product; ≥ 30 invoices including ≥ 3 credit notes and ≥ 1 foreign-currency invoice; zero wrong totals, numbering errors or data loss in the last 4 weeks; one timed full restore; proposal → agreement → invoice → receipt completed with ≥ 2 real clients. |

## Decisions

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Framework | Next.js (App Router) + TypeScript, `output: 'standalone'`. Node 24 LTS (changed from 22 by Charlie, 2026-09-29: Node 22 security support ends April 2027), pnpm workspaces, TypeScript 6.0 (typescript-eslint does not support 7 yet), all pinned. |
| Repo layout | Monorepo: `packages/core` (pure rules, interfaces, events), `apps/web` (Next.js app, worker entry, UI). `packages/extensions/*` is created in Part 3 with the first extension. |
| Boundaries | `core` imports no framework, DB or extension code. Extensions (Part 3) import only `core` types and use the `ctx` handed to them. Enforced by ESLint boundary rules + package `exports`; CI fails on violation. |
| Runtime | **One Docker image, two commands**: `web` (`next start`) and `worker` (pg-boss consumer). Identical image for SaaS and self-host. `DEPLOYMENT_MODE=saas\|selfhost` env flag. |
| Hosting (own SaaS) | Staged (see "Runtime and hosting"): laptop dev compose (Phases 0–3) → one Hostinger KVM 2 VPS (Malaysia) behind Cloudflare Tunnel, nightly encrypted backups to R2 (Phase 4 → first sale) → a managed PostgreSQL with PITR and a transaction-mode pool, provider chosen at first sale (DigitalOcean Singapore was the earlier plan). Cloudflare R2 for files. Resend for email. Rejected: Cloudflare Workers/Pages for the app, Cloudflare Containers, Neon free, Supabase free. |
| Database | PostgreSQL 17 (18 allowed; dev and tests pin 17), Drizzle ORM, plain TCP driver (`postgres`), never a provider-specific HTTP driver (pg-boss's internal `pg` pool: allowed only inside pg-boss — resolved at the v4.5 approval (Approval record item 3)). postgres.js runs with **`prepare: false`** (Spike 1). **Five roles, none with `BYPASSRLS`**: `crm_migrator` (owns schema, runs migrations, installs and upgrades pg-boss, connects directly), `crm_app` (runtime; on `audit_log`, `domain_event`, `document_artifact`, `version_retention` and the version tables it may INSERT and SELECT but never UPDATE or DELETE; minimal enqueue-only grants on `pgboss`), `crm_link_resolver` (`NOLOGIN`, owns `resolve_share_link()` only), `crm_purge` (PDPA purge only; see "Audit, logging, privacy") and `crm_worker` (the worker's pg-boss instance: fetch, complete and maintenance rights on `pgboss.*`; exact grants set in Phase 1). |
| Tenancy | `billing_account` (billing owner) → `organization` (= workspace, Better Auth) → tenant data. Workspace configuration lives in `workspace_settings` (RLS), not on `organization`. One login, many workspaces, switcher in header. Zero data shared across workspaces. RLS on every tenant table; Better Auth tables carry no RLS and are reached only through the system module. |
| Auth | Better Auth: email + password (Argon2id), TOTP 2FA with recovery codes, organization plugin, rate limiting with `storage: "database"`. Passkeys in Phase 9. Google login in Part 3. |
| Money | `bigint` minor units + per-currency exponent. Multi-currency per document. Workspace base currency; `fx_rate` (manual, default = last rate used for the pair) and base totals frozen on issue. Tax **off unless the workspace is registered**; when on, workspace default tax name/rate, per-line taxable flag and rate override (every tax rate `≥ 0`, v4.7 revision 3). Exact rules in "Money rules". **No accounting features.** |
| Approval / signature | Link issued to a named contact → email OTP → typed name + "I agree" → signature record with verified email, IP, UA, timestamp, document hash → audit certificate page in PDF. |
| Agreements | Template engine; editable starter templates with `{{field}}` placeholders; single-pass substitution, no code execution; drafted for Malaysia with `{{governing_law}}`. **Part 1 types: Service Agreement (default), MSA, SOW, Change order, Sign-off.** Pattern per customer (`service_agreement` or `msa_sow`), defaulted from the workspace setting. NDA is a later template on the same engine. Lawyer reviews final text. |
| PDF | `@react-pdf/renderer`, rendered in the worker. Canonical artifact is the frozen JSON snapshot; hash = SHA-256 of canonical JSON. PDF rendered once at first send (see PDF timing), stored, never regenerated; recorded only in `document_artifact`. Merged PDFs via `pdf-lib`. |
| Jobs / events | pg-boss in the `worker`: retries with backoff, dead-letter, cron schedules. **Domain events** written to `domain_event` (append-only history) and their pg-boss job enqueued **in the same transaction** (`send` with `{ db: fromDrizzle(tx, sql), id: event.id }`; proven by Spike 2). No outbox relay. Dispatched to core handlers; extension fan-out arrives in Part 3. |
| Extensions | **Deferred to Part 3** (Appendix A kept as design). Extension rule (three tests): it talks to an external system; it is country- or customer-specific; some workspaces must be unable to use it. Anything failing the tests is core, optionally behind a per-workspace setting. Payments split: recording payments, receipts and refunds is core; collecting money (checkout, webhooks) is an extension. |
| E-invoice | Stub only in Part 1: customer TIN/BRN/SST, `einvoice_*` fields, PDF gate, `EInvoiceProvider` interface type. `einvoice-my` is a future extension. |
| Email | Adapter: SMTP (nodemailer) or Resend, chosen by env. React Email templates. |
| Files | S3-compatible via AWS SDK v3 (S3 / R2 / MinIO), path-style toggle. Private bucket, signed URLs only. |
| Validation | Zod everywhere. One `action()` wrapper: session → active workspace (from session) → role → Zod → idempotency → handler. |
| UI | Tailwind + shadcn/ui (vendored), TanStack Table + Form, dnd-kit (block reorder), Tiptap (rich-text blocks). Client link pages mobile-first. |
| Testing | Vitest, Testcontainers Postgres, Playwright, adapter contract tests. |
| Observability | Pino JSON logs (no PII, no tokens), Sentry with scrubbing on SaaS; opt-in on self-host. `/api/health` returns DB + queue status. |
| Migrations | Run by the image entrypoint before `web` starts, as `crm_migrator` over a **direct** (not pooled) connection (the runner holds a session-level advisory lock). Migrations also install/upgrade the `pgboss` schema, create queues and apply its grants. Additive / expand-contract only. |
| Secrets at rest | Stored credentials (extension settings from Part 3) encrypted with AES-256-GCM under `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`; key rotation procedure documented (re-encrypt job). |

## Threat model

| Actor | Access | Main risks | Primary controls |
|---|---|---|---|
| Anonymous with a share link | Client link pages | Token guessing, link leakage (incl. forwarded WhatsApp messages), forged approvals, scraping | 32-byte token (hash stored), expiry, revoke, per-link + per-IP rate limits, Turnstile + OTP before sign, `no-referrer`, `noindex`, `no-store` |
| Authenticated tenant user | Own workspace(s) | IDOR into other workspaces, privilege escalation | RLS + `withWorkspace()` + role checks per action; workspace never from client input |
| Insider (staff/admin) | Workspace data | Invoice redirection (bank details, DuitNow QR), data theft, silent edits | Permission matrix, step-up 2FA on high-risk actions, owner notifications, append-only audit log, immutable issued documents, content-addressed QR |
| Extension code (Part 3) | `ctx` only | Cross-workspace access, invariant bypass, credential leakage | See Appendix A: no DB access; all writes through core `ctx`; settings encrypted; handlers run post-commit in the worker |
| Supply chain / infra | Dependencies, image, secrets | Malicious package, leaked secret, unpatched CVE | Lockfile, Renovate, `pnpm audit`, secret scan, Trivy image scan, non-root read-only container, env-only secrets |

## Scope — Part 1

Documents: Customer, Quote, Proposal, Invoice, Receipt, Credit note, Refund, Service Agreement, MSA, SOW, Change order, Sign-off.

Also: recurring invoices, CSV import of customers and contacts, pipeline board, owner notifications, dashboard v1, accounting-period export, full workspace export, workspace offboarding, customer purge. **Not in Part 1**: extension contract (Part 3, Appendix A), NDA template, customer credit balance and statement of account (after Gate A, only if own use needs them, as derived views over documents and payments, never stored balances).

## Data model (main tables)

Every tenant table carries `workspace_id` + RLS policy. **Every foreign key between two tenant tables is composite on `(workspace_id, <key>)`** (general rule in Tenancy), so every tenant table that is an FK target has a unique constraint on `(workspace_id, id)` in addition to its primary key. The composite FKs named below are the ones the money and version rules rely on. Every user-editable draft carries `version` (optimistic lock). Timestamps stored in UTC; displayed in workspace timezone.

**Accounts and workspaces**

- `billing_account` (billing owner; named to avoid Better Auth's own `account` table): owner_user_id, name, plan (`free` only in Part 1), status, `stripe_customer_id` (nullable, Part 3), limits JSON. System table, reached only through the system module.
- `organization` (= workspace; Better Auth organization plugin): Better Auth's own columns (name, slug, …) + `billing_account_id`. **No workspace settings here and no RLS** (see Tenancy).
- `member` (role: owner / admin / staff), `invitation` (email, role, token hash, expires_at): Better Auth organization plugin tables, no RLS, system module only.
- **`workspace_settings`** (tenant table, RLS; one row per workspace, `workspace_id` primary key): short code, logo key, branding (brand colour, proposal cover options), address, **country (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2)**, timezone, locale, date format, base_currency, default_currency, tax_registered (bool, default false), tax_registration_no (own SST/GST no.), own_tin, own_brn, default tax name/rate (used only when tax_registered; the rate is never below 0: Zod at settings and onboarding, and CHECK rate `>= 0`, v4.7 revision 3), tax_rounding (`rate_group`), bank details, `duitnow_qr_key` (content-addressed, see Files), email settings (from name, reply-to, bcc_self), auto_send default, `default_agreement_pattern` (`service_agreement` default | `msa_sow`; applied to new customers), require_2fa, version. A later change to `base_currency` never touches finalized documents: each keeps its own frozen `base_currency`, `fx_rate` and base totals (Money rules → "Frozen base currency", v4.6). A draft's stored `fx_rate` confirmed against the old base currency is no longer valid for send or issue after such a change (Money rules → "Draft currency change", v4.6 revision 3).
- `email_template`: event, subject, body, version.
- `notification`: user_id, kind, target_type, target_id, created_at, read_at. IDs only, no personal data; read and marked per user under `withWorkspace()`.

**Customers**

- `customer`: name, address (structured), currency default, tax_identification_no (TIN), business_reg_no, sst_reg_no, po_required, `agreement_pattern` (`service_agreement` | `msa_sow`; set from `workspace_settings.default_agreement_pattern` on creation, editable per customer — a change applies to future approvals only and is refused while the customer has an unfrozen SOW awaiting provisioning; default accepted by Charlie at the v4.5 approval, Open question 10, resolved), archived_at. Cannot be deleted once it has issued documents (archive only).
- `contact`: customer_id, name, email, phone, role, is_primary.
- CSV import (customers + contacts): upload → validate every row with Zod → preview with errors → commit in one transaction; duplicate detection by customer name and contact email.

**Catalog**

- `service`: name, default description, default unit price (minor), `currency` (ISO 4217; the currency of the default unit price, prefilled from `workspace_settings.default_currency` when the service is created; the price is always read in this currency, and a later change to the workspace default does not change existing services — v4.6; `currency` cannot be changed while a default unit price is set — Money rules → "Service currency edit", v4.6), unit, default_taxable.

**Sales documents**

- `quotation`: customer, contact, currency (changeable only while the quote holds no monetary input and no linked proposal holds a fixed-amount payment-schedule milestone — Money rules → "Draft currency change", v4.6), `fx_rate` and `fx_rate_base_currency` (nullable; the user-confirmed rate for the editable draft and the base currency it was confirmed against; cleared on every currency change; frozen into the `quotation_version` on send — v4.6 revision 3), tax name/rate (the rate is never below 0: Zod and CHECK rate `>= 0`, v4.7 revision 3), valid_until, status (draft / sent / accepted / declined / expired), version (optimistic lock of the editable draft), document discount (never below 0: Money rules → "Representation", v4.7 revision 2), draft totals, number, issued_at, current_version_id, accepted_version_id, purged_at (set only by `crm_purge`; set once, only when no version remains, never changed after, and once set no version can be inserted under it: Phase 9 requirements (1) and (2) in "Customer purge: requirements the Phase 9 plan must design and test", not final). The FK `ON DELETE` actions of `current_version_id` and `accepted_version_id`: "Version references and purge" below.
- `quotation_version` (**append-only**, no UPDATE or DELETE grants to `crm_app`; DELETE only by `crm_purge` under the purge rule — purgeable only if no invoice references it and it has no `version_retention` row, and only as far as the current-pointer rule allows: "Version references and purge" below, Phase 9 requirements (3) and (4)): quotation_id, version_no, frozen snapshot (lines, totals, tax, currency, fx_rate, base totals, `base_currency` — v4.6), snapshot_hash (its input includes `base_currency`), created_at, created_by. A new row is written each time the quote is sent — directly, or inside a proposal send — if the draft differs from `current_version_id`. Old versions are never changed, so any version a client saw or accepted stays stored; its PDF is recorded in `document_artifact`. Quote → invoice copies from `accepted_version_id`, else from `current_version_id`.
- `invoice`: optional quotation_id, optional proposal_id, optional recurring_invoice_id (set only by the recurring run when it creates the invoice, never from client input, and never changed afterwards; it marks a recurring run's draft — Money rules → "Recurring runs and `fx_rate`", v4.6 revision 7), optional `source_quotation_version_id` and `source_proposal_sent_version_id` (the exact versions it was created from; recorded at draft creation together with a `version_retention` row; composite FKs `(workspace_id, source_quotation_version_id)` → `quotation_version (workspace_id, id)` and `(workspace_id, source_proposal_sent_version_id)` → `proposal_sent_version (workspace_id, id)`), customer, contact, currency (changeable only on a draft holding no monetary input — Money rules → "Draft currency change", v4.6), fx_rate (nullable on a draft; cleared on every currency change; issue refused without a valid rate — v4.6 revision 3), `fx_rate_base_currency` (draft only: the base currency the rate was confirmed against — v4.6 revision 3), `base_currency` (ISO 4217; frozen on issue with `fx_rate` and the base totals from `workspace_settings.base_currency` at that moment — v4.6), tax (name/rate; the rate is never below 0: Zod and CHECK rate `>= 0`, v4.7 revision 3), due date, status (draft / issued / part-paid / paid / credited / overdue / void; issue refused when the computed total is `≤ 0` — Money rules step 8, v4.7, revision 3; `credited` = settled entirely by credit notes with **no net payment retained** — it may have received a payment that was later refunded; recompute rule in "Refunds and invoice status"), po_number + po_file_key, document discount (never below 0: Money rules → "Representation", v4.7 revision 2), totals, number, issued_at, sent_at, bank_details_snapshot (includes the DuitNow QR hash key when one is set), reminder settings, **einvoice_status** (`not_required` default / pending / validated / rejected / cancelled), einvoice_uuid, einvoice_validation_url, einvoice_submitted_at, frozen_snapshot (includes `currency`, `fx_rate`, base totals and `base_currency`), snapshot_hash (its input includes `base_currency`).
- `recurring_invoice`: customer, contact, currency (changeable only while the template holds no monetary input — Money rules → "Draft currency change", v4.6), line items (template; the `line_item` bounds apply, including the `tax_rate_override_bps` bound, v4.7 revision 3; the template stores no document tax rate, and if it ever stores one, the same Zod and CHECK rate `>= 0` apply), document discount (never below 0: Money rules → "Representation", v4.7 revision 2), schedule (monthly / quarterly / yearly + day), next_run_at, end_after (date or count, optional), auto_send, status (active / paused / ended), version. **No rate column** (v4.6 revision 6: revision 3's template `fx_rate` and `fx_rate_base_currency` are removed, so no confirmed rate is reused across periods). A daily job creates one invoice per due period under `withWorkspace()`, idempotent by (recurring_invoice_id, period). All money rules apply at creation. If the template currency equals the current `workspace_settings.base_currency`, the invoice gets `fx_rate = 1` and is a draft, or issued and sent when `auto_send`, unless its computed total is `≤ 0` (with the bounds, only exactly 0 occurs, v4.7 revision 3): then it stays a draft and the run moves on to the next period (Money rules → "Recurring runs and `fx_rate`" → "Zero total", v4.7 revision 2). Otherwise it is created as a draft with no `fx_rate` and is never issued or sent by the run, even with `auto_send`; a user confirms a rate on that draft and then issues and sends it (Money rules → "Recurring runs and `fx_rate`", v4.6 revision 6). Until a rate is confirmed, the draft shows a "Needs rate" badge and is counted on the dashboard (v4.6 revision 7).
- `line_item`: parent (quotation | invoice | credit_note | recurring_invoice), sort, service_id, description, qty_milli (bigint, qty × 1000; CHECK `qty_milli >= 0`, v4.7 revision 2), unit, unit_amount (minor; CHECK `unit_amount >= 0`, v4.7 revision 2: no negative line amounts, negative adjustments are made with credit notes; these CHECKs and the `line_discount_bps` CHECK hold for every parent type), `currency` (ISO 4217, not null — v4.6: the currency of every amount on the line; a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger raises unless it equals the parent's `currency`, reading the parent row `FOR SHARE` so it serialises with a parent currency change; like every line of a finalized record it never changes once the parent is issued), line_discount_bps (CHECK `line_discount_bps BETWEEN 0 AND 10000`, v4.7), taxable, tax_rate_override_bps (nullable; CHECK `tax_rate_override_bps IS NULL OR tax_rate_override_bps >= 0`, v4.7 revision 3, for every parent type; Zod refuses a negative override), line_total (minor), doc_discount_alloc (minor), tax_base (minor). A quote or invoice line created from a service prefills `unit_amount` only when the document currency equals the service currency; otherwise the user enters it (Money rules → "Service price prefill", v4.6).
- `payment`: invoice_id (composite FK `(workspace_id, invoice_id)` → `invoice (workspace_id, id)`), amount (minor), currency (must equal the invoice's currency), `fx_rate` and `base_currency` (copied from the invoice's frozen values when the payment is recorded, never editable), `base_amount` (minor units of that `base_currency`, computed when recorded — Money rules → "Base amounts for credit notes, payments and refunds", v4.6 revision 3, rule from revision 5; never negative: CHECK `base_amount >= 0`; a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger raises unless `currency`, `fx_rate` and `base_currency` equal the invoice's, the same pattern as `refund.currency`), received_at, method, reference, recorded_by, external_ref (for payment-link extensions, Part 3), voided_at, voided_by, void_reason_code (required on void; the code list is open for Charlie — Changes in v4.7 item 1), void_reason (free text, required on void). v4.7 revision 1 (owner decision 2026-10-10): CHECK `voided_at`, `voided_by` and `void_reason_code` are all null or all set. Append-only except a one-time void, allowed only when the record is wrong (see "Refunds and invoice status" → "Voiding a payment"): `crm_app` has UPDATE only on (`voided_at`, `voided_by`, `void_reason_code`, `void_reason`), and the void statement is `… WHERE id = $1 AND voided_at IS NULL`; a trigger refuses any other change (the currency trigger above stays). Voided payments do not count (`net_paid`, `B_net`, the refund caps, Dashboard v1). A refund against a voided payment is refused. A void is also refused while it would leave the invoice's non-voided refunds above the refundable excess recomputed without that payment ("Voiding a payment" step 2; v4.7 revision 2, owner decision D1).
- `receipt`: payment_id (composite FK `(workspace_id, payment_id)` → `payment (workspace_id, id)`), number, `voided_at` (v4.7 revision 1: set once, in the payment-void transaction, when its payment is voided; the number is kept — Numbering). Append-only except that one-time void: `crm_app` has UPDATE only on `voided_at`, the statement is `… WHERE id = $1 AND voided_at IS NULL`, and a trigger refuses any other change. The receipt job creates no receipt for a voided payment ("Voiding a payment").
- `credit_note`: invoice_id (composite FK `(workspace_id, invoice_id)` → `invoice (workspace_id, id)`), `currency` (ISO 4217, not null — v4.6: copied from the invoice when the credit note is created, never editable; a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger raises unless it equals the invoice's currency, the same pattern as `refund.currency`), `fx_rate` and `base_currency` (v4.6 revision 3: copied from the invoice's frozen values when the credit note is created, never editable; the same trigger raises unless both equal the invoice's), reason, line items, totals, base totals (computed on issue from the invoice's `fx_rate` — Money rules → "Base amounts for credit notes, payments and refunds", v4.6 revision 3; the base total follows the cumulative rule of revision 5 and is never negative: CHECK base total `>= 0`; the base total is the credit note's official base amount, and its base subtotal, discount and tax-group amounts are informational, v4.6 revision 6), number, frozen snapshot on issue (its base totals carry the frozen `base_currency` and `fx_rate`, which are part of the hash input — Money rules → "Frozen base currency", v4.6), status (draft / issued / void), einvoice fields as on invoice. Issuing and voiding lock the invoice first and recompute its `amount_owed` and status in the same transaction (see "Refunds and invoice status"). Issue is refused if Σ issued, non-voided credit notes including this one would exceed the invoice total, and on a `void` invoice (v4.7, owner decision 2026-10-10).
- `refund` (manual; no gateway): **`payment_id` (not null; the only source; composite FK `(workspace_id, payment_id)` → `payment (workspace_id, id)`)**, amount (minor, > 0), currency (must equal the payment's currency; CHECK via trigger on insert), `fx_rate` and `base_currency` (v4.6 revision 3: copied from the payment, which carries its invoice's frozen values, when the refund is recorded; the same insert trigger raises unless both equal the payment's), `base_amount` (minor units of that `base_currency`, computed when recorded — Money rules → "Base amounts for credit notes, payments and refunds"; never negative: CHECK `base_amount >= 0`, v4.6 revision 5), refunded_at, method, reference, reason, recorded_by, voided_at, voided_by, void_reason_code (`duplicate` | `no_money_returned` | `wrong_amount` | `wrong_payment`; required on void), void_reason, `replacement_refund_id` (composite FK `(workspace_id, replacement_refund_id)` → `refund (workspace_id, id)`, so a replacement — including a cross-invoice `wrong_payment` correction — is always in the same workspace; set on the voided row; CHECK: not null when `void_reason_code IN ('wrong_amount','wrong_payment')`, null otherwise). Append-only except a one-time void, allowed only when the record is wrong and either no money was returned or a replacement recording the actual outflow is created in the same transaction (see "Refunds and invoice status"): `crm_app` has UPDATE only on (`voided_at`, `voided_by`, `void_reason_code`, `void_reason`, `replacement_refund_id`), and the void statement is `… WHERE id = $1 AND voided_at IS NULL`; a trigger refuses any other change. Voided refunds do not count. Limits (both checked under lock, see "Refunds and invoice status"): the invoice's **refundable excess** (overpayment or credit-note excess; full net paid if the invoice is void) and Σ non-voided refunds for a payment ≤ the payment amount. Money going back after a credit note is recorded as a refund against the payment being returned, with the credit note cited in `reason`; there is no credit-note source column.

**Proposals**

- `proposal`: customer, contact, linked quotation_id (nullable; required for a milestone by amount, and guarded while the `blocks` hold one — Money rules → "Draft currency change", v4.6 revision 4), status (draft / sent / viewed / approved / declined), ordered `blocks` JSON, branding options (cover, colours from `workspace_settings`), auto_send override, current_sent_version_id, approved_sent_version_id (FK `ON DELETE` actions: "Version references and purge" below), number, purged_at (as on `quotation`).
- `proposal_sent_version` (**append-only**, no UPDATE or DELETE grants to `crm_app`; no mutable columns; DELETE only by `crm_purge` under the purge rule — purgeable only if no invoice references it and it has no `version_retention` row, and only as far as the current-pointer rule allows: "Version references and purge" below, Phase 9 requirements (3) and (4)): proposal_id, version_no, frozen snapshot of the proposal blocks, `quotation_version_id` (the exact quote version shown), snapshot_hash (covers blocks + the quote version's hash), sent_at, sent_by. "Superseded" is derived (`id ≠ proposal.current_sent_version_id`), never stored. Approval writes nothing to this table; it sets `proposal.approved_sent_version_id`.
- **Version references and purge (revision 7; owner-approved 2026-10-20 as a design target, finalised by the Phase 9 plan; Bob spec Round 18 P2; moved to the Phase 9 requirement list by the owner scope decision of 2026-10-19).** Every reference to a `quotation_version` or `proposal_sent_version` row is a composite FK on `(workspace_id, <column>)` (Tenancy) with an explicit `ON DELETE` action, none deferrable. The current best-known actions, who clears the current pointer, and the current-pointer rule for fully purged and partially retained parents are Phase 9 requirement (3) in Security → "Customer purge: requirements the Phase 9 plan must design and test" (not final); requirement (4) there records Bob's spec Round 19 P1 #1 against that rule. The migrations that create these tables (Phases 4 to 6) use the actions written there; the Phase 9 plan may change them only by a reviewed migration, before any purge runs.
- Block types: scope of work (from `service`; prices are not taken from the service here — any price comes from the linked quote's lines, which follow Money rules → "Service price prefill"), deliverables, exclusions, revisions, notes (rich text), pricing (renders linked quote live), payment schedule (milestones by % or amount; an amount is read in the linked quote's currency. While a proposal's current `blocks` hold a milestone by amount, the linked quote's currency cannot change and the proposal cannot be re-linked to a quote in another currency or unlinked. A milestone by amount cannot exist without a linked quote — Money rules → "Draft currency change", v4.6 revisions 3–4), timeline (phases with dates → Part 2).
- **Pipeline board**: proposals and quotes grouped by status (draft / sent / viewed / approved / declined). Derived from document status; no separate stage table in Part 1. Values (v4.6 revision 3): a quote or proposal that has been sent shows the frozen base totals and frozen `base_currency` of its `quotation_version` (a proposal: the quote version inside its current sent version, or its approved sent version once approved; a quote: its `accepted_version_id`, else its `current_version_id`), and base totals are grouped per frozen base currency, never summed across them. A **draft** shows a base value in the current `workspace_settings.base_currency` (step 9 formula on its draft totals, not stored) only when it holds a valid `fx_rate` (`1` when its currency equals the base currency); otherwise it shows its document currency and is excluded from base totals. A quote or proposal with `purged_at` set is not on the board; on every other sent parent the current pointer must be set (revision 7, Bob spec Round 18 P2 and Round 19 P1 #1: Customer purge, Phase 9 requirements (3) and (4), not final).

**Agreements**

- `agreement_template`: type (service_agreement / msa / sow / change_order / sign_off), version, body with `{{fields}}`, governing_law default, is_default. (`nda` is added later on the same engine.)
- `agreement`: type, customer, optional proposal, `parent_msa_id` (SOW only: the customer's MSA; null until the provisioning job links one), `parent_agreement_id` (change order and sign-off only: the SOW for `msa_sow` customers, otherwise the Service Agreement), `supersedes_id` (a correction of a signed agreement: the one it replaces), frozen rendered body, body_hash, frozen_at, status (draft / sent / signed / superseded / void), signed_at, number.
  - **Freezing**: a Service Agreement, MSA, change order or sign-off is frozen (body, hash, snapshot) when created, as in v4.4. A **SOW** is created at approval as an **unfrozen draft** (body, hash and `frozen_at` null) and is frozen by the provisioning job after it is linked; its frozen body includes the parent MSA's id, number and body hash, so the SOW the client signs names exactly the MSA they sign. CHECK: `type <> 'sow' OR status = 'draft' OR (frozen_at IS NOT NULL AND parent_msa_id IS NOT NULL)` — a SOW cannot be `sent`, signed or superseded before it is linked and frozen.
  - **Lifecycle**: `signed_at` is set once and never cleared. A **signed agreement (MSA included) is never voided and never edited**; a correction is a new agreement with `supersedes_id` pointing at it, and when the correction is signed the old one becomes `superseded` (it stays signed, and anything signed under it keeps pointing at it). Only unsigned agreements (`draft` / `sent`) may become `void`.
  - **Parent integrity** (enforced in the database, tested): composite FKs `(workspace_id, customer_id, parent_msa_id)` and `(workspace_id, customer_id, parent_agreement_id)` → `agreement (workspace_id, customer_id, id)` (unique), so a parent is always in the same workspace and for the same customer; CHECKs `parent_msa_id IS NULL OR type = 'sow'` and `(type IN ('change_order','sign_off')) = (parent_agreement_id IS NOT NULL)`; a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger checks the parent's type (`parent_msa_id` → an `msa`; `parent_agreement_id` → a `sow` or `service_agreement`). `type`, `workspace_id` and `customer_id` of an agreement are immutable (trigger), so the parent check cannot be invalidated later.
- **One open MSA per customer**: unique partial index `agreement (workspace_id, customer_id) WHERE type = 'msa' AND status IN ('draft', 'sent')` (open = unsigned and not void). This is a **backstop only**; the per-customer lock in "Agreements: creation and signing" is what prevents a new open MSA next to a signed one.
- Agreement creation, MSA linking and signing order: see "Agreements: creation and signing".

**Client links and signatures**

- `share_link`: token_hash (unique), workspace_id, target (doc type + id), document_version (e.g. `proposal_sent_version.id`), issued_to_contact_id, expires_at (default 30 days; extendable for agreements), revoked_at, view_count, last_viewed_at.
- `otp_challenge`: share_link_id, code_hash, expires_at (10 min), attempts (max 5), verified_at.
- `signature`: doc ref, typed_name, verified_email, verification_method (`email_otp`), ip, user_agent, signed_at, document_hash (append-only; the certificate PDF is recorded in `document_artifact`).

**System**

- `doc_sequence`: per workspace per doc type: pattern, reset rule, counter, last_reset_period.
- `idempotency_key`: key, action, workspace, response hash, expires_at.
- `audit_log` (append-only): actor, workspace, action, target, before/after JSON (personal-data fields stored as `"[pii]"`, except the bank account number of a bank-details change entry, stored in full: revision 12, "Authorization"), ip, created_at.
- **`auth_audit_log`** (append-only; **no RLS**, system module only; revision 8, ASK 3 = B, Charlie 2026-10-20): user_id (FK `user`), event (a closed list covering: each session issued, with the reason it was issued — a completed sign-in, the password step of a 2FA sign-in, or a rotation; the 2FA step of a sign-in; 2FA enabled; 2FA disabled; recovery codes changed; the exact names are fixed by the Phase 1 PR 4 design and recorded in `docs/DECISIONS.md`), detail JSON (fixed labels and internal IDs only: the keys `reason`, `method`, `cause`, `session_id`), ip (the client IP), created_at. **Row allow-list (Bob spec Round 26 P2):** these columns and no other; `user_id` and the client `ip` are the only personal data a row holds; every other personal datum (email, name, user agent, any other header or IP) and every secret (password, TOTP code, recovery code, TOTP secret, session token) is prohibited in a row; a further `detail` key is allowed only if it is a fixed label or an internal ID or counter and the Phase 1 PR 4 spike round adds it to the allow-list; the client `ip` stays out of application and error logs. No `workspace_id`: logins and 2FA belong to a user, not a workspace, so one event is one row, also for a user with no workspace. Requirements: each row is written atomically with what it records (no session is stored without its row, and no 2FA change commits without its row); a completed login has exactly one sign-in row; enabling 2FA, disabling 2FA and regenerating the recovery codes each end every session that existed before the change, the changer's own included, and the changer continues on a fresh session issued after the change, which has its own audit row (AUTH-07; the rule in "Authentication and sessions"); a failed change leaves the old codes and the old sessions usable; signing in with a recovery code ends none (it is a login, recorded as one together with its recovery-code change; decided by Charlie on 2026-10-22: Phase 1 plan Task 31 open point F); a regeneration and a concurrent sign-in with a recovery code are serialised: if the sign-in commits first, the regeneration ends its session; if the regeneration commits first, the old code creates no session (no session, no login row, no code of the new set consumed); a sign-in authorized from the code set before a regeneration never leaves a usable session after the regeneration commits, and the audit rows and the code consumption match the order that won (Bob spec Round 26 P1); no session obtained without the second factor survives a completed 2FA enable, also when a sign-in runs at the same time; when a database from before Phase 1 is upgraded, every session that existed before is ended in the migration that starts this audit, before it starts, and no audit row is invented for it, so every user signs in again once (decided by Charlie on 2026-10-22: open point G); no failure or interruption at any step of 2FA enrolment leaves the user locked out (the user can complete or restart enrolment without an operator; this outcome binds, and the Phase 1 PR 4 design spike chooses a recovery mechanism that meets it, reviewed by Bob as code (open point A); an operator reset or a time-based automatic disable weakens it and needs a spec revision that Charlie decides); trusted devices are off, and a trusted-device cookie issued before Phase 1 cannot skip the 2FA step; the session list returns no tokens. The spec does not fix the mechanism: Phase 1 chooses it in PR 4 and records it in `docs/DECISIONS.md`. `crm_app` holds at most INSERT; no runtime role reads, updates or deletes the table in Phase 1. Retention and erasure of its rows (`user_id`, `ip`) are first decided at the professional PDPA check before Phase 4 goes live (open point E); the Phase 9 plan designs the mechanism.
- **`version_retention`** (append-only: `crm_app` INSERT + SELECT only; `crm_purge` SELECT only): workspace_id, target (typed columns, exactly one set by CHECK: `quotation_version_id` with composite FK `(workspace_id, quotation_version_id)` → `quotation_version (workspace_id, id)`, or `proposal_sent_version_id` with composite FK `(workspace_id, proposal_sent_version_id)` → `proposal_sent_version (workspace_id, id)`), source_type (`invoice` | `approval`), source_id (the invoice id, or the proposal id for an approval), retained_at. Uniqueness uses **two partial unique indexes** (a single unique index over the two nullable target columns would let duplicates through, because NULLs are distinct): `UNIQUE (workspace_id, quotation_version_id, source_type, source_id) WHERE quotation_version_id IS NOT NULL` and `UNIQUE (workspace_id, proposal_sent_version_id, source_type, source_id) WHERE proposal_sent_version_id IS NOT NULL`; the CHECK `num_nonnulls(quotation_version_id, proposal_sent_version_id) = 1` rejects rows with neither or both targets. Inserts use `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` against the matching partial index. `source_id` is deliberately **not** an FK, because the row must outlive a deleted draft invoice; an insert trigger checks that the source invoice or proposal exists **in the same workspace** at write time. Written in the same transaction whenever a version gains an invoice reference (draft creation or issue) and **in the approval transaction** for the approved proposal sent version and its quote version (so a purge can never take a version an agreement rests on). It survives deletion of a draft invoice, so a version once referenced or approved is never purged. This is the durable "retained" flag, kept out of the version tables so they stay free of UPDATE grants.
- **`domain_event`** (append-only history and replay record): name, version, workspace_id, payload JSON (IDs and amounts only), created_at. No `relayed_at`: the job is enqueued in the same transaction.
- `workspace_export`: kind (`full` | `accounting_period`), period, requested_by, status, file_key, expires_at.
- **`document_artifact`** (append-only: `crm_app` writes by INSERT only and may SELECT; no UPDATE or DELETE; DELETE only by `crm_purge` for artifacts of purgeable versions): workspace_id, target_type (`quotation_version` / `proposal_sent_version` / `invoice` / `credit_note` / `receipt` / `agreement` / `signature`), target_id, kind (`pdf` / `merged_pdf` / `certificate_pdf`), storage_key, file_sha256, renderer_version, created_at. Unique (target_type, target_id, kind). **The only place a stored PDF is recorded**; no document or version table has a `pdf_key` column.
- pg-boss owns its own `pgboss` schema (installed and upgraded by `crm_migrator` in migrations; owner = `crm_migrator`). Its tables have **no RLS** and one shared `job_common` table holds every workspace's jobs, so job payloads hold IDs only. `crm_app` gets exactly the enqueue grants proven by Spike 2 and **cannot read job payloads** (`SELECT` on `pgboss.job` / `job_common` payload columns stays denied):
  ```sql
  GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA pgboss TO crm_app;
  GRANT SELECT ON pgboss.version, pgboss.queue TO crm_app;                -- web instance start() + queue cache
  GRANT INSERT, SELECT (id, start_after) ON pgboss.job_common TO crm_app; -- RETURNING id; start_after only if a queue has notify on
  GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION pgboss.job_now() TO crm_app;              -- PUBLIC EXECUTE is revoked by default (`ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE crm_migrator REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS FROM PUBLIC`)
  ```
  Grants are **per queue table**: a queue created with its own partition gets a new table, so migrations use `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` for `crm_migrator` or re-grant in the migration that creates the queue. `crm_worker` gets the fetch/complete/maintenance rights (measured in Phase 1).
  `crm_worker`'s exact grants are measured in Phase 1 by the method in the Phase 1 plan (Settled item 2, Task 17) and recorded in `docs/runbooks/phase-1-database-roles.md` and the privilege manifest when that PR lands; until then the starting list in the Phase 1 plan (Settled item 2) is a proposal.
- `workspace_extension` is **not** in Part 1 (Appendix A, Part 3).

### Numbering

Tokens: `{CODE}`, `{YYYY}`, `{YY}`, `{MM}`, `{SEQ:n}` + free text. Live preview in settings. Number assigned only on **issue/send** (drafts show "DRAFT") → no gaps. Period tokens use the workspace timezone. Assigned inside `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the sequence row. Pattern changes affect only new documents. Voided documents keep their number.

### Money rules (`packages/core/src/money`)

**Representation**

- Amounts: `bigint` minor units; currency exponent from a static table. No floats anywhere (DB, code, JSON — serialise as strings).
- Quantity: `qty_milli` = quantity × 1000 (3 decimal places, e.g. 1.5 h = 1500). Entry with more than 3 decimals is rejected.
- Percentages (discounts, tax rates): basis points, integer (8% = 800, 12.5% = 1250).
- Line discount: **percentage only** (`line_discount_bps`), bounded `0 ≤ line_discount_bps ≤ 10000` (v4.7; Zod and a CHECK on `line_item`). A fixed-amount discount exists only at document level.
- Document discount: either a percentage (bps) or a fixed amount (minor units), never both. Bounded (v4.7 revision 2; owner decision, Charlie 2026-10-10, D3): `0 ≤ doc_discount_bps ≤ 10000`, a fixed amount `≥ 0`, and `D ≤ subtotal` (step 3). Zod and a CHECK on every row that stores a document discount (`quotation`, `invoice`, `recurring_invoice`) enforce the two ranges. `D ≤ subtotal` depends on the lines, so it stays a server check, not a CHECK.
- No negative amounts (v4.7 revision 2; D3): `unit_amount ≥ 0` and `qty_milli ≥ 0`, by Zod and by CHECKs on `line_item`, for every parent (quotation, invoice, credit note, recurring template). With `0 ≤ line_discount_bps ≤ 10000`, no line total is below 0, and with the document-discount bounds `subtotal − D ≥ 0`. Negative adjustments are made with credit notes, never with a negative line or discount.
- No negative tax rates (v4.7 revision 3; builder correction following D3, Bob PR #17 R2 P1 #1; no new owner decision). Every tax rate is `≥ 0`:
  - the workspace default rate (`workspace_settings`);
  - the document rate on `quotation` and `invoice`, and on any other row that stores a document rate;
  - `line_item.tax_rate_override_bps`, for every parent: quotation, invoice, credit note and recurring template.

  Zod refuses a negative rate on every server entry path: settings and onboarding, quote and invoice edit, line save, quote → invoice copy and credit-note lines. A CHECK on each of those columns refuses it in storage: rate `>= 0`, or `tax_rate_override_bps IS NULL OR tax_rate_override_bps >= 0` on `line_item`. This is the same style as the `line_discount_bps` CHECK. The frozen snapshots (`quotation_version`, `invoice.frozen_snapshot` and a credit note's snapshot) copy rates only from these checked columns. So the step 6 effective rate is never below 0, each step 7 group tax is `≥ 0`, and with the amount bounds no total is below 0. No upper bound on a tax rate is set: none existed before, and none is added here. Reason: D3 says no negative amounts leave a total below 0, and a negative rate would. Bob R2's case is subtotal 100 at rate −20000 bps, which gives tax −200 and total −100. Negative tax adjustments are **not decided**. If Charlie wants them, that is a new owner decision. Until then, a tax correction is made with a credit note.
- `round()` = round half away from zero, applied to an exact rational computed with `bigint` numerator/denominator. Only the steps below round.

**Steps (in order)**

1. **Line total** per line: `line_total = round(qty_milli × unit_amount × (10000 − line_discount_bps) / (1000 × 10000))`.
2. **Subtotal** = Σ `line_total`.
3. **Document discount** `D`: fixed amount, or `round(subtotal × doc_discount_bps / 10000)`. `D ≤ subtotal`.
4. **Allocate `D` to every line pro rata** by `line_total` (taxable and exempt lines alike), largest-remainder method:
   `share_i = floor(D × line_total_i / subtotal)`; distribute the leftover `D − Σ share_i` one minor unit at a time to lines with the largest fractional remainder; ties go to the earlier line (`sort` order). Store as `doc_discount_alloc`.
