For a regeneration, the new codes, the end of the old sessions, the replacement session and their audit rows commit together or not at all, and the response that returns the new codes is the one that sets the replacement session (if that response is lost after the commit, the user still holds the password and the unchanged authenticator, signs in again and regenerates again: no lockout). Signing in with a recovery code ends none (it is a login, recorded as one together with its recovery-code change; decided by Charlie on 2026-10-22: Phase 1 plan Task 31 open point F). A regeneration and a concurrent sign-in with a recovery code are serialised: if the sign-in commits first, the regeneration ends its session; if the regeneration commits first, the old code creates no session, writes no login row and consumes no code of the new set; a sign-in authorized from the code set before a regeneration never leaves a usable session after the regeneration commits, and the audit rows and the code consumption match the order that won (Bob spec Round 26 P1). No session obtained without the second factor survives a completed 2FA enable, also when a sign-in runs at the same time. When a database from before Phase 1 is upgraded, every session that existed before is ended in the migration that starts this audit, before the audit starts, and no audit row is invented for it: every user signs in again once, and no session exists without its row from the first request (decided by Charlie on 2026-10-22: open point G). No failure or interruption at any step of 2FA enrolment leaves the user locked out: the user can complete or restart enrolment without an operator. This outcome binds; the Phase 1 PR 4 design spike chooses the recovery mechanism that meets it (open point A) and Bob reviews it as code; only a proposal that weakens it (an operator reset, a time-based automatic disable) needs a spec revision that Charlie decides. Trusted devices are not offered in Phase 1, and a trusted-device cookie issued before it cannot skip the 2FA step. The session list returns no session tokens. How this is achieved is a Phase 1 implementation choice, recorded in `docs/DECISIONS.md`. The retention period and the erasure treatment of `auth_audit_log` rows (`user_id`, `ip`) are first decided at the professional PDPA check before Phase 4 goes live (see that section); Phase 4 has no live users until they are decided. An `auth_audit_log` row holds only an allow-list: `user_id`, the client `ip`, the event, the time and a `detail` of fixed labels and internal IDs (keys `reason`, `method`, `cause`, `session_id`; a further key only if it is a fixed label or an internal ID or counter added by the Phase 1 PR 4 spike round); every other personal datum (email, name, user agent, any other header or IP) and every secret (password, TOTP code, recovery code, TOTP secret, session token) is prohibited in a row, and the client `ip` stays out of application and error logs (Bob spec Round 26 P2). Personal-data fields in before/after JSON are stored as `"[pii]"`; entries reference IDs. One recorded exception (revision 12, owner decision 2026-11-27; Bob to rule on it): the bank account number in a bank-details change entry is stored in full ("Authorization"), also where it is a sole trader's and so may be personal data; application and error logs still carry no account number. `domain_event` payloads carry IDs and amounts only; pg-boss job payloads carry IDs only.
- Pino JSON logs, no PII/tokens; Sentry scrubbing; 90-day retention on SaaS.
- PDPA (MY/SG): workspace export, customer purge path, retention statement, workspace offboarding.
- **Customer purge** (owner, step-up, Phase 9) runs under `withWorkspace()` as `crm_purge`. **Scope — narrow default, accepted by Charlie at the v4.5 approval (Open question 5, resolved):**
  - A `quotation_version` / `proposal_sent_version` is **purgeable only if no invoice (draft or issued) references it** (`invoice.source_quotation_version_id` / `source_proposal_sent_version_id`) **and it has no `version_retention` row**; revision 7 (owner-approved 2026-10-20 (Charlie)) narrows this further, never widens it: a quote version that a remaining sent version shows is kept, and a parent's current version is purged only together with all its versions (Bob spec Round 18 P2; Phase 9 requirements (3) and (4) in "Customer purge: requirements the Phase 9 plan must design and test", not final). The approval transaction writes `version_retention` rows for the approved sent version and the quote version inside it, so those (and the approval signature attached to them) are never purgeable: every agreement rests on an approval.
  - With each purged version go its `document_artifact` rows and PDF files, and the `share_link`, `otp_challenge` and `signature` rows attached to it.
  - **Agreements are not purged** (a signed agreement is a contract; unsigned ones follow their proposal's approved version, which is retained).
  - Customer and contact rows are **redacted** (personal fields replaced, row kept for referential integrity) **only if the customer has no issued documents** (invoices, credit notes, receipts, signed agreements). Customers with issued documents keep their rows unchanged (archive only).
  - `crm_purge` has DELETE only on the version tables, `document_artifact`, `share_link`, `otp_challenge` and `signature` (policies restrict it to rows the rule above allows), UPDATE only on the redacted customer/contact columns and on `quotation.purged_at` / `proposal.purged_at`, and SELECT where it must check references. No DELETE on invoices, credit notes, receipts, payments, refunds, agreements, their artifacts, `version_retention`, `audit_log` or `domain_event`. **Issued financial documents are retained** for the statutory period (~7 years; verify). The purge is audited.
  - **`crm_purge` in Phase 1 (revision 7, part (b1); owner-approved 2026-10-20 (Charlie); R8 P2 #5; TEN-02, TEN-06, TEN-07, TEN-09).** What Phase 1 builds and tests for the role, and nothing more (owner scope decision, Charlie, 2026-10-19: "A"; "Changes in v4.7" item 8):
    - *Role.* `crm_purge` exists with no `BYPASSRLS` and no superuser (`rolbypassrls` and `rolsuper` false; also no `CREATEROLE`, `CREATEDB` or `REPLICATION`). It is named in the RESTRICTIVE workspace policy of every tenant table (Tenancy, revision 7 proposal (c)), so any PERMISSIVE policy later added for it is bounded by the workspace.
    - *Connection and transaction.* It connects with its own login (Open question 8, resolved 2026-10-20). The purge runs its whole unit of work, reads, locks, deletes, redactions and its audit entry, in one `withWorkspace()` transaction with the context set transaction-locally (`set_config(…, true)`; plain `SET` and `set_config(…, false)` stay banned, TEN-09), so the workspace does not outlive the transaction.
    - *Grants in Phase 1.* INSERT on `audit_log` only, for the purge's own audit entry, with a PERMISSIVE `FOR INSERT TO crm_purge` policy whose `WITH CHECK` is the workspace predicate; no SELECT on `audit_log`, and no privilege on any other project table, project function or sequence beyond this allow-list. *Allow-list (Bob spec Round 20 P2; PostgreSQL 17 defaults):* CONNECT on the database and USAGE on schema `public` (both granted to PUBLIC by default and needed to reach `audit_log`), EXECUTE on built-in functions such as `set_config` (PUBLIC default, needed for the workspace context), INSERT on `audit_log`, and USAGE on a sequence only if `audit_log`'s id default needs one. Nothing else: no other project table, no project function, no other sequence. Project functions: migrations run `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE crm_migrator REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS FROM PUBLIC` and grant EXECUTE per function only to the roles that need it, so PostgreSQL's default PUBLIC EXECUTE does not reach `crm_purge` (Bob spec Round 21 P3); the catalog test checks effective privileges (`has_function_privilege`) and counts PUBLIC-derived ones. *Alternative for the audit entry:* `crm_app` writes it instead of `crm_purge`; that depends on how `crm_purge` connects (Open question 8, resolved: own login). Every other table in the design target below is created by a later phase's migration (the first, `customer` and `contact`, in Phase 3). Each migration that creates one of them adds that table's SELECT grant and PERMISSIVE `FOR SELECT TO crm_purge` policy exactly as the design target lists them, with their catalog test rows and a real-role isolation test in the same migration, in every phase that adds one (Phases 3 to 8, any later phase too; Bob spec Round 20 P1, Round 21 P2; TEN-08): as the actual `crm_purge` role (the same real role, connected with its own login, Open question 8 resolved in revision 8), seed the table in workspaces A and B; inside `withWorkspace(A)` the SELECT returns A's rows and none of B's, and with no workspace set it returns 0 rows. A catalog check alone does not satisfy this, and the migration does not merge without it. The Phase 9 tests cover the complete purge flow on top. The DELETE and UPDATE grants, their purge and redaction policies, the purge functions and the guard triggers are added only by the Phase 9 migrations that implement the reviewed Phase 9 design, so before Phase 9 `crm_purge` can write nothing but its audit entry.
    - *Tests (Phase 1).* **Catalog** (Testcontainers; `pg_roles`, `information_schema.role_table_grants`, `column_privileges`, `pg_policies`): `crm_purge` has `rolbypassrls` and `rolsuper` false (with the existing no-`BYPASSRLS` test for every role); its only table privilege is INSERT on `audit_log`; it holds no DELETE, UPDATE or TRUNCATE on `audit_log` or `domain_event` (with the Scalability test for every runtime role, TEN-03); its effective privileges equal exactly the allow-list above (the assertion compares against that list, not against "nothing"), so it holds no privilege on any other project table, project function or sequence, and none on a table not yet created; its only PERMISSIVE policy is the `FOR INSERT` policy on `audit_log`; and the RESTRICTIVE-policy catalog test (Tenancy) shows `crm_purge` in every tenant table's workspace policy. **Behavioural** (same tools, the real role, connected with its own login, Open question 8 resolved in revision 8): inside `withWorkspace(A)` it inserts an audit row for A, and an insert for workspace B fails the `WITH CHECK`; `SELECT` on `audit_log` fails with `42501`; with no workspace set its insert is refused; after the transaction ends, the workspace context does not remain on the connection (the role has its own login, so there is no role to switch back).
  - **`crm_purge` grants and policies — design target, finalised by the Phase 9 plan (revision 7; owner-approved 2026-10-20 (Charlie); R8 P2 #5; TEN-02, TEN-06, TEN-07).** This is the design of record for when the tables exist, owner-approved on 2026-10-20 as a design target; it is not final. The Phase 9 plan finalises it together with the requirements in "Customer purge: requirements the Phase 9 plan must design and test" below, and Bob reviews that design. Under `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` a table with no policy for `crm_purge` returns zero rows to it without an error, so a reference check could pass by seeing nothing. `crm_purge` therefore gets exactly these column-level grants, no others, and on each of these tables the RESTRICTIVE workspace policy (Tenancy, revision 7) plus a PERMISSIVE policy `TO crm_purge` for each granted command, all keyed on the same `set_config('app.workspace_id', …)` context through `withWorkspace()`. No `BYPASSRLS`.

    | Table | SELECT (columns) | Other grant | Used for |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `quotation` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `customer_id`, `current_version_id`, `purged_at` | UPDATE (`purged_at`) | Candidate parents; `FOR UPDATE` lock; current-pointer rule; mark purged |
    | `proposal` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `customer_id`, `quotation_id`, `current_sent_version_id`, `purged_at` | UPDATE (`purged_at`) | Same, locked before `quotation` (lock order) |
    | `quotation_version` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `quotation_id` | DELETE | Delete purgeable versions |
    | `proposal_sent_version` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `proposal_id`, `quotation_version_id` | DELETE | Same |
    | `invoice` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `customer_id`, `status`, `source_quotation_version_id`, `source_proposal_sent_version_id` | — | "No invoice (draft or issued) references it"; "customer has issued documents" |
    | `version_retention` | `workspace_id`, `quotation_version_id`, `proposal_sent_version_id` | — | "No retention row" |
    | `agreement` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `customer_id`, `status`, `signed_at` | — | "Customer has a signed agreement" |
    | `customer` | `id`, `workspace_id` and the redacted columns | UPDATE (the redacted columns) | Redaction |
    | `contact` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `customer_id` and the redacted columns | UPDATE (the redacted columns) | Redaction |
    | `share_link` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `target_type`, `target_id`, `document_version` | DELETE | Links of purged versions |
    | `otp_challenge` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `share_link_id` | DELETE | Challenges of those links |
    | `signature` | `id`, `workspace_id`, the doc ref columns (type, id, version) | DELETE | Signatures of purged versions |
    | `document_artifact` | `id`, `workspace_id`, `target_type`, `target_id`, `storage_key` | DELETE | Artifacts and their files |
    | `audit_log` | none | INSERT | The purge's own audit entry, in the purge transaction |

    - *Policy predicates.* Every `crm_purge` policy carries the workspace predicate. The SELECT policies add nothing else. The UPDATE policies on `quotation` and `proposal` add nothing else (PostgreSQL applies the UPDATE policy's `USING` to `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` as well, and the lock must be taken on every candidate parent before the check); the purge rule for those rows is Phase 9 requirement (1) (`purged_at` guard). The UPDATE policies on `customer` and `contact` add the redaction rule: no `invoice` of that customer has a status other than `draft`, and no `agreement` of that customer has `signed_at` set. The DELETE policies add the purge rule through one `SECURITY INVOKER` SQL function, `purge_version_is_purgeable(type, id)`: (a) no `invoice` row names the version as its source; (b) no `version_retention` row targets it; (c) for a `quotation_version`, no remaining `proposal_sent_version` names it in `quotation_version_id` (the job deletes sent versions before quote versions, so a sent version it deleted earlier in the transaction no longer counts); and (d) if the version is its parent's current version, every other version of that parent meets (a) to (c) (Bob spec Round 18 P2: Phase 9 requirement (3), current-pointer rule). Bob's spec Round 19 P1 #1: (d) checks only that the other versions meet (a) to (c), not that they are gone, so it does not stop a direct `DELETE` of a current version from leaving a live parent with a NULL current pointer; that is Phase 9 requirement (4), still open. On `quotation_version` and `proposal_sent_version` it is applied to the row itself; on `document_artifact`, `share_link`, `otp_challenge` (through its link) and `signature` the target must be a `quotation_version` or `proposal_sent_version` for which it holds (for `document_artifact`, also a `signature` whose doc ref is such a version). Because the function runs as `crm_purge` under RLS, it is only as good as the SELECT policies on `invoice`, `version_retention`, the version tables and their parents; the Phase 9 catalog test below guards that.
    - *Issued documents.* Credit notes, payments and receipts are reached through their invoice: each belongs to an invoice of the same customer (composite FKs) that is no longer a draft, so the `invoice.status` check covers them, and `crm_purge` does not read those tables. *Alternative:* also grant SELECT on `credit_note` (`invoice_id`, `status`), `payment` (`invoice_id`) and `receipt` (`payment_id`) and check them directly, at the cost of a wider read set. The Phase 9 behavioural test (1) (requirement (6)) fails if the assumption is wrong.
    - *Must not read:* any other table: `line_item`, `payment`, `refund`, `receipt`, `credit_note`, `recurring_invoice`, `service`, `workspace_settings`, `email_template`, `notification`, `doc_sequence`, `idempotency_key`, `workspace_export`, `agreement_template`, `domain_event`, `audit_log` (INSERT only), the Better Auth tables, `billing_account` and `pgboss.*`; and on the tables above, no snapshot, body, hash, amount, total or token-hash column. (The audit-entry alternative is under "`crm_purge` in Phase 1".)
    - *Catalog test for the design target (Phase 9; the SELECT rows are added by each migration that creates a listed table, the rest by the Phase 9 migrations; the Phase 1 part is under "`crm_purge` in Phase 1"):* `crm_purge`'s table and column privileges (`information_schema.role_table_grants`, `column_privileges`) equal the table above exactly; it holds no privilege on any other table, schema object or function except `purge_version_is_purgeable`; each listed table has a PERMISSIVE policy `TO crm_purge` for each granted command and for no other command; `rolbypassrls` is false; `quotation` and `proposal` each carry the `purged_at` guard trigger, and `purge_guard_purged_at()` is not `SECURITY DEFINER`; `quotation_version` and `proposal_sent_version` each carry the insert guard trigger, and `purge_guard_parent_not_purged()` is not `SECURITY DEFINER`; every FK that references a version table has the `ON DELETE` action of Phase 9 requirement (3) (`confdeltype`, and `confdelsetcols` for the two `SET NULL` pointers, in `pg_constraint`), and `crm_purge` holds no UPDATE on `quotation.current_version_id` or `proposal.current_sent_version_id` (each added by the migration that creates the table).
  - **Race with invoicing:** creating a draft invoice from a quote or a proposal's payment schedule takes `FOR SHARE` on the parent `quotation` / `proposal` row, records the source version ids on the invoice and inserts the `version_retention` row(s) in the same transaction; issuing takes the same `FOR SHARE` locks, re-checks that the source versions exist and inserts the retention rows if missing (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). The purge job takes `FOR UPDATE` on each candidate parent row (`proposal` before `quotation`, per the lock order) and re-checks references and retention **inside that lock** before deleting anything, then sets `purged_at` only on a parent with no version left (Phase 9 requirement (1)). Issuance and purge on the same version therefore serialise: either the invoice reference exists first and the version is kept, or the version is gone first and the draft cannot be created or issued from it. The lock is on the parent, not the version row, because `FOR SHARE` / `FOR UPDATE` need UPDATE privilege and nobody holds UPDATE on version tables.
- **Workspace offboarding** (owner, step-up, Phase 9): full export first, then deletion; what is retained follows the statutory retention answer (Open question 6).

#### Customer purge: requirements the Phase 9 plan must design and test (revision 7; not yet fully designed)

**Status of this subsection.** Owner scope decision (Charlie, 2026-10-19: "A"; "Changes in v4.7" item 8): the customer-purge rules are a requirement list for the Phase 9 plan, a gate list that is not designed to completion and not reviewed to completion. Bob's Rounds 17-19 findings on the purge are recorded here as requirements; the Phase 9 plan must close them and Bob must review that design; this spec does not claim they are solved. Each requirement gives what must hold, then the current best-known approach, which is **not final**: the Phase 9 plan may replace it with another design that meets the requirement and its tests. The function and trigger names (`purge_version_is_purgeable()`, `purge_guard_purged_at()`, `purge_guard_parent_not_purged()`) and the test numbers are kept so the review history still lines up. The accepted narrow scope ("Customer purge" above, Open question 5) does not change, and nothing here widens it. Gate: "Next steps after approval" → Current gates (f).

1. **`purged_at` guard (Bob spec Round 17 P1).** *Requirement:* a quotation's or proposal's `purged_at` goes only from NULL to non-NULL, only as `crm_purge`, only when no version of that parent remains, and is immutable once set, enforced at the database for every role, not only by the job. *Current best-known approach (not final):* A quotation or proposal is marked purged only when **none of its versions remains**. When any of its versions is not purgeable (an invoice, draft or issued, references it, or it has a `version_retention` row; for a proposal this always includes its approved sent version), the purge deletes the purgeable versions with their attached rows and leaves the parent's `purged_at` NULL. Reason: the narrow scope above purges versions, not the parents that invoices and approvals rest on; such a parent is still a live, retained record, and marking it purged would let a read that filters on `purged_at` hide it. A parent that never had a version holds nothing protected and may be marked. In this approach the rule is enforced at the database, not only by the job: a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger on `quotation` and one on `proposal` call one `SECURITY INVOKER` function, `purge_guard_purged_at()`. When `purged_at` is unchanged it does nothing. Otherwise it raises `42501` (the row is unchanged and the transaction aborts) unless all three hold: (1) the old value is NULL and the new value is not, so once set `purged_at` never changes and never returns to NULL, for every role; (2) `current_user` is `crm_purge`, so `crm_app` and every other role is refused whatever its grants; (3) no `quotation_version` row with that `quotation_id` remains (for a quotation), or no `proposal_sent_version` row with that `proposal_id` (for a proposal). Because `crm_purge` can delete only purgeable versions (the DELETE policies in the design target above), (3) can hold only once every version of the parent was purgeable and deleted, so it needs no separate proof that the deletes ran; an `UPDATE` as `crm_purge` with no workspace context matches no row (RLS), and one inside a `withWorkspace()` transaction before the versions are gone is refused. The function runs as `crm_purge` under RLS and sees the parent's versions through the SELECT policies on the version tables (same workspace by the composite FK); the Phase 9 catalog test guards those policies. PostgreSQL checks EXECUTE on a trigger function only when the trigger is created, so `crm_purge` needs no grant on it. `crm_purge`'s UPDATE grant on these tables stays `purged_at` only, so it cannot change any other column (`42501` from the grant). *Lock order.* In its one `withWorkspace()` transaction the purge job takes `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the candidate `proposal` rows, then on the `quotation` rows; re-checks references and retention under those locks; deletes the purgeable `proposal_sent_version` rows with their attached rows, then the purgeable `quotation_version` rows with theirs; then runs `UPDATE … SET purged_at = now()` only on the locked parents it found with no version left, and the trigger re-checks (3) while the row lock is held. No version can appear under a locked parent: inserting a `quotation_version` or `proposal_sent_version` takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on the parent in the insert guard (requirement (2)), which waits for `FOR UPDATE` and then refuses the insert if the purge set `purged_at`; drafting or issuing an invoice takes `FOR SHARE` ("Race with invoicing" above).
2. **Insert guard under a purged parent (Bob spec Round 18 P1).** *Requirement:* no `quotation_version` or `proposal_sent_version` is inserted under a parent whose `purged_at` is set, enforced at the database, including an insert that waited on the purge's lock and runs after the purge commits; the app refuses first with the neutral message. *Current best-known approach (not final):* No version is inserted under a purged parent. A `BEFORE INSERT` trigger on `quotation_version` and one on `proposal_sent_version` call one `SECURITY INVOKER` function, `purge_guard_parent_not_purged()`. These are the only tables whose rows hang directly under a purgeable parent: `share_link`, `document_artifact` and `signature` hang under a version, and their same-workspace insert trigger (Tenancy) already refuses a target version that no longer exists. The function runs `SELECT purged_at FROM quotation WHERE workspace_id = NEW.workspace_id AND id = NEW.quotation_id FOR KEY SHARE` (for a sent version, `proposal` and `NEW.proposal_id`) and raises `42501` (nothing is written and the transaction aborts) if `purged_at` is not NULL; if it finds no row it does nothing, and the composite FK refuses the insert (`23503`). *Why it sees the purge's committed state:* `FOR KEY SHARE` conflicts with the purge's `FOR UPDATE`, so while the purge holds the parent the trigger waits; under READ COMMITTED (PostgreSQL's default; no transaction here sets another level), when the purge commits, PostgreSQL follows the row to its newest committed version, locks that and returns it, so the trigger reads the `purged_at` the purge set. If the purge left `purged_at` NULL (a version was kept), the insert goes ahead. Under REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE the same wait ends in `40001`, which also refuses the insert. The lock is taken in the trigger because the FK check runs only after the row is inserted and checks only that the parent exists. If the trigger takes its lock first, the purge's `FOR UPDATE` waits for the send to commit, and its reads under the lock then see the new version: the purge rule applies to it like any other version, and the `purged_at` guard sees it. `FOR KEY SHARE` on the parent is the lock the FK check takes anyway, so the trigger adds no new lock and no new lock order; a locking read needs UPDATE privilege on the table, which `crm_app` holds on both parents. The function runs as the inserting role (`crm_app`) under RLS and reads only the parent row in its own workspace; as for `purge_guard_purged_at()`, no EXECUTE grant is needed. *In the app:* every send path (quote send, proposal send, Resend) reads `purged_at` under its parent lock and refuses before inserting; the trigger is the database backstop. Either way the owner sees the neutral message "This document is no longer available", with nothing about what was purged, and the send writes nothing (no version, link, event or email).
3. **Version FK `ON DELETE` actions and the current-pointer rule (Bob spec Round 18 P2).** *Requirement:* every reference to a version has a defined `ON DELETE` action, and a fully purged parent and a partially retained parent each have one defined outcome for `status`, the current pointer and `purged_at`.
   *Current best-known approach (not final):* Every reference to a `quotation_version` or `proposal_sent_version` row is a composite FK on `(workspace_id, <column>)` (Tenancy) with this `ON DELETE` action, none deferrable:

   | Reference | `ON DELETE` | Why |
   |---|---|---|
   | `quotation.current_version_id` | `SET NULL (current_version_id)` | Cleared only when the purge deletes every version of the quote (current-pointer rule below) |
   | `proposal.current_sent_version_id` | `SET NULL (current_sent_version_id)` | Same, for the proposal |
   | `quotation.accepted_version_id` | `NO ACTION` | An accepted version is what the client accepted; the approval transaction retains it (`version_retention`), so the purge never deletes it. Direct quote acceptance (gated before the Phase 5 plan) must retain it the same way |
   | `proposal.approved_sent_version_id` | `NO ACTION` | Retained by the approval transaction; never purgeable |
   | `proposal_sent_version.quotation_version_id` | `NO ACTION` | A remaining sent version keeps the quote version it showed; the purge skips a quote version a remaining sent version names |
   | `invoice.source_quotation_version_id`, `invoice.source_proposal_sent_version_id` | `NO ACTION` | Never purgeable (invoice reference) |
   | `version_retention.quotation_version_id`, `version_retention.proposal_sent_version_id` | `NO ACTION` | Never purgeable (retention row) |
   | `quotation_version.quotation_id`, `proposal_sent_version.proposal_id` | `NO ACTION` | The purge never deletes a parent |

   The column list on `SET NULL` is required: the FK is composite, and a plain `SET NULL` would also set `workspace_id` to NULL and fail its NOT NULL (column lists on `SET NULL` exist since PostgreSQL 15; the project runs 17). Under `NO ACTION` a delete that would break the reference fails and the whole purge transaction rolls back; the purge rule (`purge_version_is_purgeable()`, "`crm_purge` grants and policies — design target") keeps the purge from trying, so `NO ACTION` is only a backstop. `share_link`, `document_artifact` and `signature` reach a version through polymorphic references (Tenancy), not FKs: the purge deletes them with their version, and their same-workspace insert trigger refuses a row whose target version no longer exists.
   - *Who clears the pointer.* PostgreSQL runs a referential action as the owner of the referencing table (`crm_migrator`), not as the role that ran the `DELETE`, and referential integrity checks bypass row security (PostgreSQL docs, "Row Security Policies"). So `crm_purge` needs no UPDATE grant on the pointer columns, and its UPDATE grant on `quotation` and `proposal` stays `purged_at` only. The action's UPDATE fires the parent's `BEFORE UPDATE` triggers; `purge_guard_purged_at()` does nothing because `purged_at` is unchanged. The first point comes from PostgreSQL's implementation rather than its documentation, so the Phase 1 plan confirms it with a test on PostgreSQL 17 (a `DELETE` as `crm_purge`, which holds no UPDATE on the pointer, clears it).
   - *Current-pointer rule.* The purge deletes a parent's current version (`quotation.current_version_id`, `proposal.current_sent_version_id`) only when it deletes every version of that parent. So a purge has one of two outcomes per parent: (i) **every version purgeable**: all are deleted, the FK action clears the current pointer, and the purge sets `purged_at` (requirement (1)). `status` is not changed (it keeps its last value, e.g. `sent` or `declined`), and `accepted_version_id` / `approved_sent_version_id` are already NULL, because a version they name is retained. A parent with `purged_at` set is a tombstone: it is not on the pipeline board or in document lists and search, every owner action on it (edit, send, Resend, create invoice) is refused with the neutral message "This document is no longer available", its client links were deleted with its versions, and no version can be inserted under it (requirement (2)). (ii) **at least one version kept** (an invoice references it, it has a `version_retention` row, or a remaining sent version names it): the purge deletes the other purgeable versions but keeps the current version, whatever its own state, so a purge run by the job leaves no dangling or NULL pointer on a live parent (a direct `DELETE` outside the job is requirement (4), still open); `purged_at` stays NULL and the parent keeps its status and its place on the board. Reason: it is the smallest rule that keeps every live parent's current pointer valid, with no new UPDATE grant and no change to which version the client was last sent; the kept current version holds the same customer data as the retained version beside it. *Alternative:* delete a purgeable current version and repoint the parent to its newest retained version in the purge transaction; that needs a `crm_purge` UPDATE grant on the pointer columns and a guard on the repoint, and the board and "Changed since sent" would then show a version the client was not last sent. The job is meant to follow this rule through condition (d) of `purge_version_is_purgeable()`; Bob's spec Round 19 P1 #1 shows that (d) does not enforce it against a direct `DELETE` as `crm_purge`, so the database-level rule is requirement (4) below, still open.
4. **No live parent with a NULL current pointer after a direct `DELETE` (Bob spec Round 19 P1 #1; new in this revision).** *Requirement:* no committed parent with `purged_at` NULL has a NULL current pointer (`quotation.current_version_id`, `proposal.current_sent_version_id`) once that pointer has been set. This invariant must be enforced at the database boundary for a direct `DELETE` as `crm_purge`, outside the purge job, including a parent with no other version; or deletion must be redesigned so that only one atomic, guarded purge operation can remove a current version. *Why the current text does not meet it:* `crm_purge` has direct DELETE on both version tables, the pointer FKs are `SET NULL`, and condition (d) of `purge_version_is_purgeable()` checks only that the other versions meet (a) to (c), not that they were deleted. So inside `withWorkspace(A)` a direct `DELETE` of the only current version, or of the current version first while eligible older versions remain, passes (a) to (d); the FK clears the pointer, nothing sets `purged_at`, and requirement (1)'s guard sees an unchanged NULL. *Current best-known approaches (not final; the Phase 9 plan picks one and Bob reviews it):* (i) a constraint trigger on `quotation` and on `proposal`, `AFTER UPDATE`, `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`, that fires when the current pointer goes from non-NULL to NULL and, at commit, re-reads the row and raises unless `purged_at` is set; with requirement (1) this means a committed NULL pointer implies that every version is gone and the parent is a tombstone; or (ii) no direct DELETE grant on the version tables for `crm_purge`, and one purge function per parent that locks, checks and deletes in one statement, with its own reviewed privilege design. *Tests:* (13) direct-role DELETE as `crm_purge` inside `withWorkspace(A)`, outside the purge job: (a) a current-only parent: deleting its only, current, purgeable version without setting `purged_at` is refused (at the statement or at commit), and the version and its pointer are intact; (b) a parent whose other versions are all eligible: deleting the current version first is refused in the same way, and nothing changes; (c) the same parents purged by the job (every version deleted, then `purged_at` set, in one transaction) commit; after each case, a query finds no committed parent with `purged_at` NULL whose current pointer was set and is now NULL.
5. **`pdf.render` racing the purge (Bob spec Round 19 P1 #2; EVT-05; new in this revision).** *Requirement:* once a purge of a version has committed and every `pdf.render` job for that version has finished, no PDF object for that version is left in object storage, including an upload that was in flight when the purge ran and any retry of that job. A render job whose version is missing because it was purged has a defined terminal outcome: it finishes (completed, not retried, not dead-lettered, no "failed, retry" badge) and logs IDs only. Serialisation between purge and render, or compensating cleanup, is designed in the Phase 9 plan. *Why the current text does not meet it:* `pdf.render` is enqueued on a send and runs in the worker ("PDF timing", "Domain events and jobs"); a render can read the version, wait, and upload after the purge deleted the version, its `document_artifact` rows and its files; its artifact INSERT is then refused (the target is gone), so the new object has no row for the purge to find, the general failed-job path retries (and can upload again) and dead-letters, and the orphan sweep deletes such an object only after 24 hours. *Current best-known approach (not final):* (i) before rendering and again before uploading, the job checks in a short `withWorkspace()` transaction that the version exists (taking `FOR KEY SHARE` on its parent, which waits for a purge holding `FOR UPDATE`), and if it is gone it finishes with the terminal outcome above; (ii) if its artifact INSERT is refused because the target version is gone, it deletes its own upload and finishes with the same terminal outcome, never as a retryable error; (iii) a crash between upload and that delete leaves an orphan for the sweep, so the Phase 9 plan either shortens the sweep for a purged version (for example a per-version key prefix that the purge, or a delayed cleanup job it enqueues, lists and deletes after the longest render window) or states why the 24-hour sweep meets the PDPA purge promise. A per-version key prefix changes the PDF key layout of "PDF timing" and would have to be set before Phase 4 stores its first PDF, or be migrated. *Tests:* (14) with object storage (MinIO) and the pg-boss queue, forced with hooks: (a) render first reads the version and pauses before upload, the purge commits, the render resumes: no object for the version, no artifact row, the job completed and not retried; (b) render uploads and pauses before its artifact INSERT, the purge commits, the render resumes: the INSERT is refused, the object is deleted, the job completed; (c) as (b) with a simulated crash after upload and a retry: the retry ends with the terminal outcome and, after the defined cleanup, no object remains; (d) the purge commits before a queued render starts: the job ends with the terminal outcome and uploads nothing.
6. **Minimum test list (Phase 9).** The Phase 9 plan may add tests and may not drop any of these. **Behavioural and lock-interleaving** (Phase 9; Testcontainers with the real `crm_purge` role, rows seeded in two workspaces): (1) a customer with an issued invoice, with a credit note, with a receipt or with a signed agreement is not redacted, through the job and through a direct `UPDATE` as `crm_purge` (0 rows); (2) a version referenced by a draft invoice, or with a `version_retention` row, is not deleted by a direct `DELETE` as `crm_purge` (0 rows), and neither are its artifacts, links, OTP challenges and signatures; (3) `SELECT` as `crm_purge` on `payment`, `line_item`, `audit_log` or a snapshot column fails with `42501`; (4) under `withWorkspace(A)` it reads, updates and deletes nothing of workspace B, and with no workspace set it sees 0 rows; (5) an unreferenced, unretained version and its attached rows are deleted; (6) under `withWorkspace(A)`, a direct `UPDATE … SET purged_at = now()` as `crm_purge` is refused (`42501`, row unchanged) on a quotation whose version a draft invoice references, on one whose version an issued invoice references, on one whose version has a `version_retention` row, on a proposal with an approved sent version, and on a proposal whose sent version an invoice references; it is also refused on a parent whose versions are all purgeable but not yet deleted; on a parent with a retained current version and a purgeable older one, the job deletes the purgeable one and leaves `purged_at` NULL (a purgeable current version is test (12)); (7) on an eligible parent the same `UPDATE` succeeds only inside the purge's `withWorkspace()` transaction after its versions are deleted there, and with no workspace context it updates 0 rows; (8) once `purged_at` is set, an `UPDATE` to NULL or to another value is refused as `crm_purge` and as `crm_app`, and setting it on an eligible parent as `crm_app` is refused; an `UPDATE` of any other `quotation` or `proposal` column as `crm_purge` fails with `42501`; (9) forced interleaving, purge first (Bob spec Round 18 P1): run once for a quotation and once for a proposal, the purge holds `FOR UPDATE` on the eligible parent and a send that inserts its version (a `quotation_version`, or a `proposal_sent_version`) waits in the insert guard; the purge deletes the versions, sets `purged_at` and commits; the waiting INSERT then fails with `42501`, the send's transaction writes nothing (no version, link, event or job), and the parent ends with `purged_at` set, its current pointer NULL, no version under it and no other column changed; an invoice draft started the same way waits on its `FOR SHARE`, then finds no source version and is refused; (10) direct INSERT after purge: as `crm_app` inside `withWorkspace()`, an INSERT of a `quotation_version` under a purged quotation and of a `proposal_sent_version` under a purged proposal each fail with `42501`, and the app's send and Resend show "This document is no longer available" and write nothing; (11) inverse order: a send commits a new current version first, then the purge runs (its `FOR UPDATE` waits for the send if the send still holds its lock): on a parent that also keeps a retained version, the new current version is kept by the current-pointer rule, `purged_at` stays NULL (the `purged_at` guard sees remaining versions, and a direct `UPDATE … SET purged_at` is refused) and the version and its pointer are intact; on a parent with no retained version the new version is purgeable like any other and is deleted with its link, and the parent is marked purged; (12) current pointer (Bob spec Round 18 P2): (a) the only version is current and purgeable: it is deleted, `current_version_id` / `current_sent_version_id` become NULL through the FK action although `crm_purge` holds no UPDATE on them, `purged_at` is set, `status` and every other column are unchanged, and the parent is off the pipeline board with its owner actions refused; (b) a retained older version (an invoice reference, and separately a `version_retention` row) beside a purgeable current version: the job keeps the current version with its artifacts and links, its pointer is unchanged, `purged_at` stays NULL, and a direct `DELETE` of that current version as `crm_purge` deletes 0 rows; (c) a protected current version (an invoice references it, or it has a `version_retention` row, including an approved sent version and its quote version) is never deleted, through the job or a direct `DELETE`, and `current_*`, `accepted_version_id` and `approved_sent_version_id` stay set; (d) a quote version named by a remaining sent version is not deleted (0 rows), and no purge in tests (1) to (12) fails on a `NO ACTION` FK. Together with: (13) from requirement (4); (14) from requirement (5); the issue-vs-purge concurrency test on the same version ("Race with invoicing" above); the purge-vs-approval test in both orders ("Approval and Resend concurrency" → "Purge vs approval"); and the catalog test for the design target above.

### Backups

- Phase 4 → first sale (VPS): nightly encrypted `pg_dump` + bucket sync to R2 (separate bucket and credentials from the app), monthly restore test.
- From first sale: the managed PostgreSQL's PITR (provider chosen at first sale), plus the nightly encrypted dump to R2.
- Self-host: daily encrypted `pg_dump` + bucket sync, off-site copy.
- Restore tested in Phase 4 (Gate A), drilled in Phase 9 and in the self-host guide; one timed full restore for Gate D.

## Reliability

- Money, approval and event writes, and their job enqueue, in single DB transactions.
- Idempotency keys on issue / approve / pay / refund / convert / sign; recurring invoice creation idempotent by (recurring_invoice_id, period).

### Lock order

Every transaction that locks more than one of these rows follows **exactly one** of two named chains (a path may skip entries, never reorder them); several rows of the same kind are locked in `id` order.

**Chain A — proposal chain** (anything that touches a proposal):

1. `invoice`
2. `payment`
3. `proposal`
4. `share_link` of the proposal
5. `quotation`
6. `customer`
7. `agreement` — the SOW, Service Agreement, change order or sign-off being signed, provisioned, voided or superseded
8. `share_link` of that agreement (for a supersede: the voided SOWs' links, then the correcting MSA's own link, each in `id` order)
9. `agreement` — the MSA (signed through the SOW's link, or superseded / superseding)

**Chain B — quote chain** (direct quote acceptance and any other quote-only action, no proposal involved):

1. `invoice`
2. `payment`
3. `quotation`
4. `share_link` of that quotation
5. `customer`
6. `agreement` rows (as A7)
7. `share_link` of that agreement (as A8)
8. `agreement` — the MSA (as A9)

**Rules that keep the chains consistent:**
- After its entry point, B takes the same order as A (`quotation` before `customer` before all agreement rows); only the position of the entry `share_link` differs.
- The `share_link` rows of the two chains are **different rows** (a proposal link vs a quotation link), so A's "proposal link before quotation" and B's "quotation before its own link" never order the same pair of rows two ways.
- An action that starts in chain B **never** takes a `proposal` or a proposal's `share_link`. Any action that touches both a proposal and a quotation follows chain A. **Mixing the chains in one transaction is forbidden.**
- **Future quote actions** (anything reached from a quotation's own link or a quote-only screen) follow chain B: `quotation` before its own `share_link`.
- **Quote currency change** (v4.6 revision 3, Money rules → "Draft currency change"): with linked proposals it follows chain A — the linked `proposal` rows `FOR SHARE` (`id` order), then `quotation FOR UPDATE`; with none, chain B (`quotation` only). Linking, re-linking or unlinking a proposal (v4.6 revision 4) locks `proposal FOR UPDATE`, then the currently linked and the target `quotation` rows `FOR SHARE` in `id` order (chain A). It checks the milestone currency rule only after both are held. A draft quote's hard delete takes the quote-currency-change locks.
- Why A puts the proposal's `share_link` before `quotation`: the proposal link is the entry point of every client action on a proposal (it is resolved, locked and rechecked first), and the quotation is then a dependent row reached through the proposal's sent version.
- `customer` sits after the proposal/quotation rows (approval and quote acceptance take it `FOR SHARE` last, to read the pattern) and before all agreement rows (provisioning, MSA signing, supersede and pattern changes take it `FOR UPDATE` first). `invoice` → `payment` come first in both chains: invoice creation and issue then take `proposal` / `quotation` `FOR SHARE` (purge race), and payments and refunds never lock anything after `payment`, except that a payment void then updates that payment's `receipt` row (v4.7). A payment void takes `invoice FOR UPDATE` → `payment FOR UPDATE`, as a refund does. The receipt job takes `payment FOR SHARE` before it creates a receipt, so it serialises with a payment void. A refund on another payment of the same invoice also takes `invoice FOR UPDATE` first, so it serialises with a payment void too (v4.7 revision 2). Credit-note issue and void also take `invoice` first (entry 1) and lock nothing from the chains after it, so they serialise with payments and refunds on the same invoice.
- Tested: a direct quote acceptance running concurrently with a proposal approval for the same customer — no deadlock, no lost update, both transactions commit their own writes (see Verification).

### Approval and Resend concurrency

Approve, decline, Resend, revoke and proposal edits all run in one transaction under `withWorkspace()` and take row locks in chain A above: **`proposal` → `share_link` → `quotation`** (approval then `customer` `FOR SHARE`). No path takes them in another order, so they serialise without deadlock.

**Approve / decline** (client, by link):

1. Resolve the token (read-only, outside the lock) to get `workspace_id`, `share_link_id`, `target_id`.
2. `SELECT … FROM proposal WHERE id = target_id FOR UPDATE`.
3. `SELECT … FROM share_link WHERE id = share_link_id FOR UPDATE`.
4. Recheck **inside the lock**: `revoked_at IS NULL`, `expires_at > clock_timestamp()` (wall-clock time read **after** the locks are held — never `now()`, which is frozen at transaction start), `share_link.document_version = proposal.current_sent_version_id` (and that sent version row still exists — a purge deletes the version and its links together), `proposal.approved_sent_version_id IS NULL`, status ∈ {sent, viewed}, OTP verified for this link within 10 minutes of `clock_timestamp()`. Any failure → roll back, show the generic "link not available" page (or "already approved" for a repeat by the same verified signer, via the idempotency key).
5. `SELECT … FROM quotation … FOR UPDATE`, then `SELECT agreement_pattern FROM customer … FOR SHARE`; write approval, acceptance, signature, the **`version_retention` rows** (`source_type = 'approval'`) for the approved proposal sent version and its quote version (no extra lock: this transaction already holds `proposal` and `quotation`, the parents the purge must lock), **exactly one** agreement (a frozen Service Agreement, or an unfrozen draft SOW with `parent_msa_id` null; no MSA is read, created or locked here), `domain_event` rows (incl. `agreement.created`) and their jobs (the agreement email only for a Service Agreement); commit.

**Resend / revoke** (owner): lock `proposal FOR UPDATE`, then the current `share_link FOR UPDATE`; refuse if `approved_sent_version_id IS NOT NULL` ("Already approved — create a new quote or change order") or if `purged_at` is set ("This document is no longer available": Customer purge, Phase 9 requirement (2), not final); then lock `quotation FOR UPDATE`; otherwise insert the new quote version (if changed) and the new sent version, set `current_sent_version_id`, revoke the old link, insert the new link; commit. The UI then offers email (sent automatically) and "Share new link to WhatsApp".

The same pattern applies to agreement signing (lock `agreement` → `share_link`; for MSA + SOW, `customer` first — see "Agreements: creation and signing") and to direct quote acceptance, which follows chain B (`quotation` → its own `share_link` → `customer` `FOR SHARE`).

**Race test (required, Testcontainers, two real connections as `crm_app`):** start approve and Resend on the same proposal concurrently, many iterations with randomised delays. Exactly one wins every time. If Resend wins: approve fails with the generic page, no signature or acceptance row exists. If approve wins: Resend is refused, the old link stays the approved version, no new sent version exists. Also: two concurrent approvals of the same link → one signature row. **Expiry-under-lock test:** hold the proposal lock in one connection, let the link's `expires_at` pass, then release; the waiting approval must fail with the generic page. The race test runs against the simplified approval transaction (one agreement, no MSA work) and must still pass.

**Purge vs approval** (both lock `proposal` `FOR UPDATE` first, so they serialise): if approval commits first, its `version_retention` rows exist and the purge, rechecking inside its lock, skips those versions. If the purge commits first and deleted the current sent version, it and its `share_link` are gone: the approval's step 3 finds no link row (or step 4 finds no sent version) and it fails cleanly with the generic "link not available" page, writing nothing. If the current-pointer rule kept the current sent version (Customer purge, Phase 9 requirement (3), not final), its link is kept too and the approval runs as usual. Test required (both orders forced).

### Agreements: creation and signing

Each customer has `agreement_pattern` (`service_agreement` | `msa_sow`), defaulted from `workspace_settings.default_agreement_pattern`.

1. **In the approval transaction** (`proposal` → `share_link` → `quotation` → `customer FOR SHARE`): create exactly one agreement. `service_agreement` → a Service Agreement, frozen at creation and (if auto-send) its email enqueued here, as in v4.4. `msa_sow` → a SOW as an **unfrozen draft** with `parent_msa_id` null. The transaction never reads, creates or locks an MSA, and never enqueues an email for a SOW. It commits and emits `agreement.created`.
2. **Provisioning job on `agreement.created`** (worker, under `withWorkspace()`, job `id` = event id; runs only for a SOW that is still an unfrozen draft), all in one transaction:
   1. `SELECT … FROM customer WHERE id = $customer FOR UPDATE` — the **per-customer lock**, shared with MSA signing and supersede.
   2. `SELECT … FROM agreement WHERE id = $sow FOR UPDATE`; if it is already frozen, or no longer a draft (voided), stop — a retried job does nothing.
   3. **Re-check under the customer lock**: if the customer has a current signed MSA (`type = 'msa' AND status = 'signed'`, i.e. signed and not superseded), link to it and create nothing. Otherwise, if an open MSA exists, link to it. Only otherwise `INSERT` a new frozen draft MSA (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the one-open-MSA index — a backstop that should never fire under the lock) and link to it.
   4. `UPDATE agreement SET parent_msa_id = $msa … WHERE id = $sow AND parent_msa_id IS NULL`, then **freeze the SOW**: render its body with the MSA's id, number and body hash embedded, set `body_hash`, snapshot and `frozen_at`.
   5. If auto-send: set the SOW (and a new MSA) to `sent`, create the share link and enqueue the email for the pair in the same transaction (job `id` derived from the SOW id, so a retry cannot send twice). "Share to WhatsApp" becomes available only now.
   6. Commit. A job that fails at any step rolls back entirely and is retried; the SOW stays an unfrozen draft (not sendable, not signable — the CHECK on `agreement` forbids it) until a run succeeds; a dead-lettered job shows "failed, retry" on the SOW.
   The partial unique index alone would allow "one signed MSA + one new open MSA" (job checks, finds none, a signing commits, job inserts); the customer lock plus the re-check in step 3 is what prevents it.
3. **Signing link** (`share_link` targets the frozen SOW; the public read model includes its parent MSA): if the MSA is unsigned, the client signs the MSA first, then the SOW, in the same OTP session.
   - **Signing the MSA** locks `customer` → `agreement` (SOW) → `share_link` → `agreement` (MSA), sets the MSA `signed`/`signed_at`; if the MSA has `supersedes_id`, the old MSA becomes `superseded` in the same transaction (see 4).
   - **Signing the SOW** locks `agreement` (SOW) → its `share_link` and, **under those locks**, rechecks and **refuses unless**: the SOW status is `sent` (not `void`, `superseded` or already `signed`), it is frozen, `parent_msa_id` is set and that MSA's `signed_at IS NOT NULL`, and the share link is not revoked and `expires_at > clock_timestamp()`. A refusal shows the neutral "This link is not available" page, the same as any revoked link. Holding the SOW's lock is sufficient: the SOW's status, `parent_msa_id` and frozen body cannot change while it is locked (provisioning and supersede take the same lock), the link cannot be revoked while it is locked (supersede takes it next), and `signed_at` is set once and never cleared — a signed MSA can be superseded but **never voided, unsigned or edited** — so the checks cannot be invalidated before commit.
4. **Correcting a signed agreement** (owner): a new agreement is created with `supersedes_id` = the signed one; the signed one stays `signed` until the correction is signed, then becomes `superseded`. A correcting MSA is sent on its own link (`share_link` targeting the MSA). **Signing it (the supersede transaction)**, in chain A order: `customer FOR UPDATE` → the SOWs frozen against the old MSA (`id` order) `FOR UPDATE` → their `share_link` rows, then the correcting MSA's own link (`id` order) → the MSA rows. Under those locks it marks the old MSA `superseded`, **voids every unsigned SOW** frozen against the old MSA (it names the old MSA's hash) and **revokes each voided SOW's share link** (`revoked_at = clock_timestamp()`) in the same transaction, so no live link to a voided SOW can exist. **An already signed SOW is left completely unchanged**: its status stays `signed`, its `parent_msa_id` still points at the old MSA it was signed under, and its link is not touched. Sign-SOW vs supersede: if the SOW signing holds the SOW lock first, it commits `signed` and the supersede then finds it signed and leaves it alone; if the supersede holds it first, it voids the SOW and revokes the link, and the waiting signing's recheck refuses. Never a SOW signed after its MSA was superseded, and never a live link to a voided SOW (test, both orders). The owner then regenerates each voided SOW ("Regenerate SOW" creates a new draft SOW from the same approved proposal and emits `agreement.created`, which links it to the new MSA). **Signed** SOWs keep pointing at the MSA they were signed under. New SOWs always link to the customer's current signed, non-superseded MSA. Events: `agreement.superseded`.
5. **Change orders and sign-offs** reference, via `parent_agreement_id`, the SOW for `msa_sow` customers and the Service Agreement otherwise (parent integrity: see Data model → Agreements).
6. **Pattern change** (default accepted by Charlie at the v4.5 approval, Open question 10, resolved): updating `customer.agreement_pattern` takes the customer lock and is refused while the customer has an unfrozen draft SOW (provisioning pending). It affects future approvals only; existing agreements are unchanged. Approval reads the pattern under `FOR SHARE` on the same row, so the two serialise.

### Refunds and invoice status

- Recording a payment: `invoice FOR UPDATE`, refuse if the invoice is `void` or `credited` (nothing is owed; a credited invoice first needs a credit-note void to owe money again), insert the payment, recompute the invoice status; commit.
- **Refund eligibility** (accepted by Charlie at the v4.5 approval, Open question 11, resolved; a broader policy would be a new owner decision): all amounts in the invoice currency (payments, refunds and credit notes carry it),
  - `net_paid = Σ non-voided payments − Σ non-voided refunds` (v4.7)
  - `amount_owed = invoice_total − Σ issued credit notes`
  - `refundable_excess = max(0, net_paid − amount_owed)`; if the invoice is `void`, `refundable_excess = net_paid`.
  - A refund is allowed only if `amount ≤ refundable_excess` **and** `Σ non-voided refunds on this payment + amount ≤ payment.amount`.
- **Recording a refund**: `invoice FOR UPDATE` (the payment's invoice) → `payment FOR UPDATE` (lock order, both chains); check the refund currency equals the payment currency and refuse if the payment is voided (v4.7; this includes a replacement in a refund void); **re-sum inside the lock** both the invoice's `refundable_excess` and the payment's refunds and refuse if either limit would be exceeded; insert the refund; recompute the invoice status; emit `refund.recorded`; commit. **Voiding a refund** is allowed **only when the refund record is wrong**, never to reverse money actually returned and never to make a credit-note void possible. A void must leave the recorded refunds equal to the money that actually left the account: **either no money was returned for this record, or a corrected replacement refund recording the actual outflow is created in the same transaction** (Andy proposal, for Bob confirmation). The owner picks a required `void_reason_code` and gives a free-text `void_reason`:
  - **`duplicate`** or **`no_money_returned`**: the owner must confirm **"No money was returned for this record."** No replacement is created (`replacement_refund_id` stays null). A `duplicate` void removes only the duplicate; the retained amount changes by exactly that record.
  - **`wrong_amount`** or **`wrong_payment`**: money **was** returned but recorded wrongly. The owner must confirm **"Money was returned; I am recording the correct refund below"** and enter the replacement (correct amount and/or correct payment; same currency rule: currency = that payment's currency). The replacement is inserted in the **same transaction** and its id is set as `replacement_refund_id` on the voided row (not null for these two codes). A `wrong_amount` / `wrong_payment` void without a replacement is rejected.
  - **Locks and caps**: the void and any replacement run under the refund lock order — `invoice FOR UPDATE` → `payment FOR UPDATE`. If the replacement is against a different payment, both payments are locked in `id` order; if that payment belongs to a different invoice, both invoices are locked first in `id` order, then both payments. A correction across invoices is allowed **only within the same workspace** (the replacement's payment, both invoices and both refunds must share `workspace_id`). The guarantee comes from the **composite workspace FKs** on refund → payment `(workspace_id, payment_id)`, payment → invoice `(workspace_id, invoice_id)` and refund → refund `(workspace_id, replacement_refund_id)`, so a refund can never point at another workspace's payment even if the application passes a foreign ID; RLS is the second layer. Inside the locks the per-payment cap and the refundable-excess cap are **re-summed with the voided record excluded and the replacement included**, for every affected payment and invoice. If the replacement fails any cap check, the whole transaction rolls back and **nothing is voided**.
  - **Same transaction**: set `voided_at` / `voided_by` / `void_reason_code` / `void_reason` / `replacement_refund_id` once (`WHERE voided_at IS NULL`), insert the replacement, recompute every affected invoice's status, write an audit entry (who, when, reason code and text, before/after of both refunds and of the invoice status), emit `refund.voided` and, for the replacement, the normal `refund.recorded`.
  - The credit-note void guard is unchanged: because a wrong-amount or wrong-payment correction always keeps the actual outflow on record, a refund void never lowers the money actually returned unless no money was returned.
- **Voiding a payment** (v4.7 revision 1; owner decision, Charlie 2026-10-10; the details are the builder's specification, for Bob's review). A payment is voided only when its record is wrong, for example a wrong amount, a duplicate or the wrong invoice. The correct payment, if there is one, is then recorded as a new payment under the normal rule. An Admin or Owner with step-up (Authorization) picks a required `void_reason_code` and gives a free-text `void_reason`. All of it runs in one transaction under `withWorkspace()`:
  1. `invoice FOR UPDATE` → `payment FOR UPDATE` (lock order entries 1–2, as for a refund).
  2. Inside the lock, refuse if the payment is already voided, or if **any non-voided refund exists against it**. The message is: "This payment can't be voided while refunds recorded against it stand." Then apply the **refund guard** (v4.7 revision 2; owner decision, Charlie 2026-10-10, second round: D1 "Refuse that void"). Inside the invoice lock, with the invoice's payments, refunds and credit notes **re-summed there**, `payments_total_without_p = Σ non-voided payments of the invoice other than this one`, `refunds_total = Σ non-voided refunds on the invoice` and `amount_owed` as in "Refund eligibility" above:
     `refundable_excess_without_p = max(0, payments_total_without_p − amount_owed)`; if the invoice is `void`, `refundable_excess_without_p = payments_total_without_p`.
     If `refunds_total > refundable_excess_without_p`, the void is **refused** with: "This payment can't be voided while refunds recorded on this invoice stand: voiding it would leave more refunded than the client has paid beyond what they owe." The message does **not** suggest voiding a refund: a recorded refund may be money already returned, and voiding its record does not recover it. The guard's result can change later only through genuine events: an additional genuine payment on the invoice, or a wrong refund record corrected under the refund-void rules ("Voiding a refund"). A refund is voided first only if its record is wrong, never to make a payment void possible. The comparison uses gross payments, as in the credit-note void guard: `net_paid` already has the refunds subtracted, so comparing refunds against `max(0, net_paid − amount_owed)` without the payment would count them twice. This is the refundable-excess cap of "Refund eligibility" applied to the invoice as it would be after the void; the per-payment cap of refunds on other payments is not changed by the void. Because `amount_owed ≥ 0`, an allowed void leaves `refunds_total ≤ payments_total_without_p`, so `net_paid` never falls below 0.
  3. Set `voided_at` / `voided_by` / `void_reason_code` / `void_reason` once (`WHERE voided_at IS NULL`). If the payment has a receipt, set the receipt's `voided_at` once (`WHERE voided_at IS NULL`). Its number is kept.
  4. Recompute `net_paid` without the voided payment, and the invoice status by the rule below. `B_net` loses exactly the payment's stored `base_amount`. No other row changes, and nothing frozen is edited (Money rules → "Voids", "Payment voids").
  5. Write an audit entry (who, when, reason code and text, before/after of the payment, its receipt and the invoice status). Emit `payment.voided`, plus `invoice.paid` / `invoice.credited` / `invoice.overdue` when the status becomes one of those. Commit.
  - **Status:** unlike a refund, a payment void lowers `net_paid` and can move a `paid` invoice to `part-paid`, `issued` or `overdue` (or to `credited` when the credit notes cover the whole total). A `void` invoice stays `void`, and its refundable excess (`net_paid`) falls by the payment. As after a credit-note void, reminder history is kept, and the scan resumes with the next reminder step not yet sent.
  - **Receipt and client notice:** a job on `payment.voided` emails the invoice's contact a void notice naming the receipt number, only if a receipt was created for the payment. The voided receipt keeps its number and stored PDF and is shown as void. The receipt job (flow step 10) takes `payment FOR SHARE`. For a payment that is already voided, it creates no receipt, uses no number and sends nothing. So a void that commits before the receipt exists leaves no receipt and sends no void notice.
  - Refunds and a payment void on the same payment serialise on `invoice` → `payment`. If a refund commits first, the void is refused. If the void commits first, the refund is refused. A refund on another payment of the same invoice also takes `invoice FOR UPDATE` first, so it serialises with the void (v4.7 revision 2, D1). If that refund commits first, the void re-sums it in step 2 and is refused when the refunds exceed the recomputed excess. If the void commits first, the refund's refundable-excess check runs without the voided payment. A payment void never changes a refund row.
- **Invoice status** is recomputed inside the invoice lock in each of these transactions (and in credit-note issue and void, below, and in a payment void, above):
  1. `void` stays `void` (never recomputed).
  2. If `amount_owed > net_paid`: `part-paid` when `net_paid > 0`, otherwise `issued` or `overdue` by due date.
  3. If `amount_owed ≤ net_paid`: `paid` when `net_paid > 0`; **`credited`** when `net_paid = 0` (only possible when issued credit notes cover the whole total, which is never `≤ 0` because an invoice whose computed total is `≤ 0` cannot be issued, v4.7 revision 3 — settled entirely by credit with **no net payment retained**; the invoice may have received a payment that was later refunded).
  `credited` is never overdue, gets no reminders, is excluded from receivables ("outstanding" / "overdue"), and never counts as paid (see Dashboard v1). A payment against a `credited` invoice is refused; no refund is possible (`net_paid = 0`, so the refundable excess is 0); a voided credit note moves it back to `issued` / `overdue` by this rule. Entering `credited` emits `invoice.credited` (as entering `paid` emits `invoice.paid`). Because a refund can only take away the excess, **a refund never moves an invoice from `paid` to `part-paid`, `issued` or `overdue`** (it can move `paid` to `credited` when a fully credited invoice's money is returned in full): a fully paid, unadjusted invoice has no excess and cannot be refunded, so no overdue reminder can restart because of a refund. (Voiding a refund can only raise `net_paid`. Voiding a payment lowers it and can reopen the invoice; see "Voiding a payment", v4.7.) Reminders and the daily overdue scan read the recomputed status, never a cached one.
- Because concurrent refunds on one invoice all lock the same `invoice` row first, two refunds that each fit the excess (or the payment cap) alone cannot both succeed.
- Money returned after a credit note is a refund against the payment, with the credit note named in `reason`; it fits the excess rule because the credit note reduced `amount_owed`.
- **Issuing or voiding a credit note** changes `amount_owed`, so each runs in **one transaction** that takes `invoice FOR UPDATE` first (lock-order entry 1 in both chains; nothing else is locked after it except the credit note row itself), **re-sums inside the lock** payments, non-voided refunds and non-voided issued credit notes, recomputes `net_paid`, `amount_owed` and the invoice status (`paid` / `credited` / `part-paid` / `issued` / `overdue`, by the rule above), and writes the status change, the credit note change (issue: number and frozen snapshot; void: status `void`, number kept) and their domain events (`credit_note.issued` or `credit_note.voided`, plus `invoice.paid` / `invoice.credited` / `invoice.overdue` when the status becomes one of those) in the same transaction. Payments, refunds and credit notes on one invoice therefore serialise on the `invoice` row.
  - **Issue lowers `amount_owed`.** If it falls to or below `net_paid` and a net payment is retained (`net_paid > 0`), the invoice becomes `paid`; if no net payment is retained (`net_paid = 0`, the credit notes cover the whole total), it becomes **`credited`**, not `paid`. Either way an `overdue` invoice stops being overdue and no further reminders are sent; any `net_paid − amount_owed` is refundable under the excess rule. If `amount_owed` stays above `net_paid`, the invoice stays `part-paid` (or `issued` / `overdue` when nothing is paid) with a smaller balance, and reminders show the new balance.
  - **Cap on issue** (v4.7 revision 1; owner decision, Charlie 2026-10-10). Inside the invoice lock, with the issued, non-voided credit notes re-summed there, issue is **refused** unless `credit_note.total ≤ amount_owed_before`. Equivalently, Σ issued, non-voided credit notes including this one ≤ `invoice_total`. Issue is also refused on a `void` invoice. A credit note for exactly the remaining amount is allowed. Concurrent credit notes on one invoice serialise on the `invoice` row, so together they cannot exceed the total. Like the refund caps, this is a server rule under the lock; it sums several rows, so it is not a CHECK. With the cap, `0 ≤ amount_owed ≤ invoice_total` always holds, which the identities in Money rules → "Base amounts for credit notes, payments and refunds" rely on. A credit-note void raises `amount_owed` and cannot break the cap. A paid invoice can still be credited and then refunded under the excess rule.
  - **Void raises `amount_owed`** — **guarded** (refusal accepted by Charlie at the v4.5 approval, Open question 13, resolved; allowing the void anyway would be a new owner decision). Inside the invoice lock, with `payments_total = Σ non-voided payments` (v4.7), `refunds_total = Σ non-voided refunds` and `owed_without_cn = invoice_total − Σ issued credit notes other than this one`:
    `refundable_excess_without_cn = max(0, payments_total − owed_without_cn)`.
    If `refunds_total > refundable_excess_without_cn`, the void is **refused** with: "This credit note can't be voided while refunds paid out against it stand: voiding it would leave the client owing money that was already returned." The message does **not** suggest voiding the refund: a recorded refund may be money already returned, and voiding its record does not recover it. The guard's result can change later only through genuine events: an additional genuine payment on the invoice, or a wrong refund record corrected under the refund-void rules (a `duplicate` / `no_money_returned` void, or a `wrong_amount` / `wrong_payment` void whose replacement records a smaller or differently allocated actual outflow; see "Voiding a refund"). A refund is never voided to make a credit-note void possible. Otherwise the void goes ahead. The comparison uses gross payments: `net_paid` already has the refunds subtracted, so comparing refunds against `max(0, net_paid − owed_without_cn)` would count them twice and wrongly refuse a void when the refund returned a genuine overpayment. For a `void` invoice the rule is unchanged (its refundable excess is `net_paid`, independent of credit notes), so the guard never refuses there. When allowed, a previously `paid` or `credited` invoice becomes `part-paid`, or `issued` / `overdue` by due date when nothing is net paid. Reminders and the daily overdue scan read the recomputed status. **Reminder history is kept** (sent reminders stay in the audit log and on the invoice's reminder record); the scan resumes with the next reminder step not yet sent, computed from the due date, and does not resend earlier steps.

### Jobs and errors

- pg-boss: retries with exponential backoff, dead-letter; "failed, retry" badge in UI; enqueue idempotent by event id (job `id` = `domain_event.id`).
- Friendly user messages; details in logs and Sentry.
- `/api/health` checks DB and queue.

## Scalability

- `web` stateless → N replicas; `worker` scales independently.
- App connections go through the pooled connection string; the pooler **must be transaction mode**, and postgres.js runs with `prepare: false` (non-overridable when `DATABASE_URL` points at a pooler). Spike 1: only `prepare: true` with a pooler that has `max_prepared_statements=0` broke (~60% of transactions failed with `26000`, no leak); Drizzle already runs unprepared, so the flag only affects raw tagged calls, and DO's pooler settings are not under our control. Migrations always go **direct** (session-level advisory lock, DDL). pg-boss uses advisory *transaction* locks, so it works through the pooler with polling; LISTEN/NOTIFY needs a **direct** connection (through the pooler the channel registration is lost and it falls back to polling). PgBouncer on self-host when needed.
- Starting pool sizes per process (Spike 1/2; correctness was proven at 2, throughput is not): web postgres.js `max` 5–10 via the pooler; web pg-boss `max: 2`; worker pg-boss `max` 3–5; PgBouncer `default_pool_size` sized to the DB plan.
- Composite indexes: `(workspace_id, created_at)`, `(workspace_id, status)`, `(workspace_id, customer_id)`.
- Numbering lock serialises only one workspace.
- `audit_log`, `domain_event`, share-link views: **no archive job in Part 1** (revision 7; owner-approved 2026-10-20 (Charlie); Bob R11 option 1 adopted; UNI-10). This replaces the earlier "monthly archive job when they grow", which had no role or mechanism and would have needed a DELETE grant on append-only tables. No role deletes rows from `audit_log`, `auth_audit_log` or `domain_event` (the purge rule's exclusions stand), and share-link views are counters on the `share_link` row (`view_count`, `last_viewed_at`) plus audit entries, so they add no table of their own. Reason: at 5–15 person agencies these tables stay small for years, and the `(workspace_id, created_at)` indexes keep reads fast. *Alternative (Bob R11 option 2):* create `audit_log` and `domain_event` as monthly range partitions on `created_at`, and let `crm_migrator` detach (never delete) old partitions in a reviewed runbook; never row deletes by a runtime role. Choosing it later means a table rewrite, because a table cannot be turned into a partitioned one in place. Test (TEN-03): a Phase 1 grant catalog test that no runtime role (`crm_app`, `crm_worker`, `crm_purge`, `crm_link_resolver`) and not `PUBLIC` holds DELETE, UPDATE or TRUNCATE on `audit_log`, `auth_audit_log` or `domain_event`; only the owner `crm_migrator` holds them, by ownership.
- Plan limits become columns on `billing_account` in Part 3.
- PDF, recurring invoices and (from Part 3) all extension work happen in the worker.

## Known risks

- **R1 empty-string setting — confirmed and fixed.** Spike 1 reproduced it (`22P02` without `nullif`, 0 rows with it). Guarded by the required `nullif`, the policy lint/test and RLS tests that seed rows in other workspaces.
- **R2 `pg` driver.** pg-boss depends on `pg` and opens its own node-postgres pool in the worker and, small (`max: 2`), in the web-side send-only instance; this conflicts with the "`postgres` driver only" rule in the Phase 0 plan. Resolved at the v4.5 approval (Approval record item 3): `pg` is allowed only inside pg-boss, and all app queries stay on postgres.js.
- **R3 `pgboss` tables have no RLS — mitigated.** Job rows from every workspace share `job_common`. Payloads hold IDs only; `crm_app` has enqueue-only grants and cannot read payloads (Spike 2). Grants are per queue table, so a new partitioned queue needs `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` or a re-grant in its migration.
- **R4 connection budget.** Each process opens the app pool (postgres.js) and pg-boss's pool; Better Auth uses the app pool. Small managed PostgreSQL plans (DigitalOcean's was the earlier example) allow a few tens of connections, and migrations (and LISTEN, if ever enabled) need direct connections. Starting sizes are under Scalability; final sizes in the Phase 1 plan.
- **R5 pins.** pg-boss releases often and its Drizzle adapter targets drizzle 1.0 RC; pin pg-boss 12.35.0 exactly and keep drizzle-orm 0.45.3 until a planned upgrade (Spike 2 proved `fromDrizzle` on 0.45.3).
- **R6 untested by the spikes:** pg-boss maintenance/supervise through the pooler over a full cycle (60 s+); the exact grants of `crm_worker` (the spike used the schema owner as worker); a run against the chosen managed PostgreSQL provider's pooler. The first two are checked in Phase 1 before the worker role is fixed, against a local PgBouncer in transaction mode. The provider run cannot happen in Phase 1 (the provider is chosen at first sale): it is the **pre-cutover gate** in "Runtime and hosting" Stage 3, and a Phase 1 done-when does not wait for it.
- **R7 web queue cache lag.** The web pg-boss instance caches queue options for up to 60 s (`queueCacheIntervalSeconds`); a queue option changed after start is not seen until refresh. Queue options (including `notify`) change only in migrations, followed by a restart. With `notify` on, a backlog tail can wait for the 30 s backstop poll; `notify` stays off in Phase 1.

## Runtime and hosting

- Image: multi-stage Dockerfile → Next.js standalone + worker entry. Entrypoint runs migrations, then `web` or `worker`.
- Dev compose: `web`, `worker`, `postgres`, `minio`, `mailpit`.
- **Stage 1 — Phases 0–3: laptop.** Dev compose only; no hosting.
- **Stage 2 — Phase 4 → first sale: one Hostinger KVM 2 VPS (Malaysia)** running prod compose (`web`, `worker`, `postgres`, `cloudflared`), files on R2, email via Resend. Cloudflare in front (DNS + proxy, Tunnel so the VPS exposes no inbound ports, R2, Turnstile). Nightly encrypted backups to R2. VPS provider: Hostinger KVM 2, chosen by Charlie on 2026-10-15 (Open question 4, resolved).
- **Stage 3 — from first sale: a managed PostgreSQL, provider and region chosen at first sale** (DigitalOcean Managed PostgreSQL, Singapore was the earlier plan; the choice must consider the Malaysia location of the app and the PDPA cross-border check) (PITR, pool port in transaction mode for the app with `prepare: false`, direct port for migrations and for any LISTEN/NOTIFY user).
